### SESSION 5 — MODERNISATION THEORY: FIVE ASSUMPTIONS AND FOUR HOSTILE OBJECTIONS

#### The picture first

```text
   MODERNISATION AS A THEORY -- NOT AS NEUTRAL INEVITABILITY
   =========================================================

   WHAT IT DESCRIBES (real, and not to be denied)
     industrialisation | urbanisation | mass education | occupational
     differentiation | bureaucratic capacity | scientific rationality |
     expanding markets
        -- a TRANSITION from "traditional" to "modern" social organisation
                                |
                                v
   THE FIVE ASSUMPTIONS THAT MAKE IT A THEORY RATHER THAN A DESCRIPTION
   +---+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | 1 | LINEAR SEQUENCE    | societies move through broadly comparable |
   |   |                    | stages                                    |
   +---+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | 2 | CONVERGENCE        | institutional differences DIMINISH as     |
   |   |                    | productive and administrative capacity    |
   |   |                    | grows                                     |
   +---+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | 3 | TRADITIONAL/MODERN | inherited authority and local production  |
   |   | CONTRAST           | YIELD to rational, mobile, differentiated |
   |   |                    | institutions                              |
   +---+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | 4 | DIFFUSION          | capital, knowledge and technology can     |
   |   |                    | TRANSMIT development                      |
   +---+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | 5 | EXPERT DIRECTION   | planners can identify obstacles and       |
   |   |                    | ACCELERATE the transition                 |
   +---+--------------------+------------------------------------------+
                                |
             THE FOUR HOSTILE OBJECTIONS -- ONE PER ASSUMPTION CLUSTER
                                |
     +-------------+-------------+-------------+--------------------+
     | DEPENDENCY  | POST-       | CULTURAL /  | MARXIST            |
     |             | DEVELOPMENT | GANDHIAN    |                    |
     +-------------+-------------+-------------+--------------------+
     | denies that | exposes the | rejects     | asks WHO OWNS      |
     | underdevel- | POWER TO    | HOMOGENIS-  | PRODUCTION and     |
     | opment is   | DEFINE      | ATION of    | WHO APPROPRIATES   |
     | merely an   | others as   | the good    | THE GAINS          |
     | EARLIER     | DEFICIENT   | life        |                    |
     | STAGE       |             |             |                    |
     +-------------+-------------+-------------+--------------------+
     | hits (1)(2) | hits (5)    | hits (3)    | hits (2)(4)        |
     +-------------+-------------+-------------+--------------------+
                                |
     AND ONE MORE THAT SCRIPTS ALWAYS FORGET
     AMBEDKARITE: does modernisation DISMANTLE graded hierarchy, or does
     it merely REORGANISE it inside new occupations and institutions?
                                |
                                v
   THE VERDICT TO STATE -- BALANCED BUT DECISIVE
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | MODERNISATION NAMES REAL STRUCTURAL TRANSFORMATIONS BUT IS NOT A   |
   | NEUTRAL OR INEVITABLE LAW. ITS INSTITUTIONS COUNT AS PROGRESS ONLY |
   | WHEN THEY ENLARGE EQUAL CAPABILITY, DEMOCRATIC AGENCY AND          |
   | ECOLOGICAL SECURITY.                                               |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: modernisation split into what it accurately describes and the five assumptions that turn the description into a theory; the four hostile objections mapped onto the assumptions each one attacks; the Ambedkarite objection that scripts routinely omit; and the verdict that concedes the structural transformations while denying the neutrality and the inevitability.*

#### In plain words

Modernisation theory is the position most candidates hold without knowing they hold it. Whenever a script says that a society is "still traditional", or that it must "catch up", or that development can be delivered by transferring technology and expertise, it is using modernisation assumptions — and using them as though they were descriptions of how the world works rather than a theory that can be false.

The way to take control of this material is to separate the **description** from the **assumptions**. The description is largely accurate: industrialisation, urbanisation, mass schooling, occupational differentiation, bureaucratic capacity, scientific rationality and expanding markets are real processes and denying them is not available. What converts that description into a theory is a set of five further claims: that societies pass through broadly comparable **stages**; that institutional differences **converge** as capacity grows; that inherited authority and local production **yield** to rational, mobile, differentiated institutions; that capital, knowledge and technology can be **diffused**; and that **experts** can identify obstacles and accelerate the transition.

⚠️ Each of the four standard critiques attacks a different assumption, and saying which one is attacked is the difference between an organised answer and a list of complaints. Dependency attacks the stage and convergence claims by arguing that underdevelopment is produced rather than inherited. Post-development attacks the expert-direction claim by asking who authorised the diagnosis. The cultural and Gandhian critique attacks the traditional/modern contrast by refusing to concede that one form of the good life is simply superior. The Marxist critique attacks convergence and diffusion by asking who owns production and who takes the surplus. And the Ambedkarite objection — the one scripts forget — attacks the entire package by asking whether modern institutions **dismantle** graded hierarchy or merely **reorganise** it into new occupations and new offices.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **What modernisation theory says.** It interprets development as a transition from "traditional" to "modern" social organisation through industrialisation, urbanisation, education, occupational differentiation, bureaucratic capacity, scientific rationality and expanding markets.

✅ **Its five assumptions.** (1) **Linear sequence**: societies move through broadly comparable stages. (2) **Convergence**: institutional differences diminish as productive and administrative capacity grows. (3) **Traditional/modern contrast**: inherited authority and local production yield to rational, mobile and differentiated institutions. (4) **Diffusion**: capital, knowledge and technology can transmit development. (5) **Expert direction**: planners can identify obstacles and accelerate transition.

✅ **The four hostile objections.** Dependency denies that underdevelopment is merely an earlier stage. Post-development exposes the power to define others as deficient. Cultural and Gandhian critiques reject homogenisation of the good life. Marxist criticism asks who owns production and appropriates the gains.

✅ **The fifth objection, and it is Indian.** Ambedkarite criticism asks whether modernisation **dismantles** or merely **reorganises** graded hierarchy. ⚠️ This is the objection most likely to be omitted and the one that gives an Indian answer analytical rather than illustrative content.

⚠️ **The verdict.** Modernisation names real structural transformations but is **not a neutral or inevitable law**. Its institutions count as progress only when they enlarge **equal capability, democratic agency and ecological security**.

⚠️ **Why the verdict must be two-sided.** Denying the structural transformations is empirically indefensible and will read as ideology; accepting them as inevitable and neutral concedes the entire critical apparatus of this clause. The examinable position holds both: the transformations are real, and their evaluation is open.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The five assumptions, named** ✅ — the move that converts "modernisation is Eurocentric" from a slogan into an argument, because it identifies exactly what is being denied.
- **The objection-to-assumption mapping** ⚠️ — dependency against sequence and convergence, post-development against expert direction, cultural and Gandhian critique against the traditional/modern contrast, Marxist critique against convergence and diffusion.
- **The Ambedkarite question** ✅ — whether modern institutions dismantle graded hierarchy or reorganise it; a bounded bridge, used as a *test* of modernisation and never expanded into caste doctrine.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Modernisation describes real structural transformations, but it becomes a theory only through five further assumptions — linear sequence, convergence, the traditional/modern contrast, diffusion and expert direction — and every serious critique of it is an attack on one of those five rather than a denial that industrialisation occurred.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**linear sequence** · **convergence** · **traditional/modern contrast** · **diffusion** · **expert direction** · **produced underdevelopment** · **authority to diagnose** · **homogenisation of the good life** · **who appropriates the surplus** · **reorganised rather than dismantled hierarchy**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Modernisation is a theory rather than a neutral description of what happens when societies industrialise, and identifying the five assumptions that make it a theory is the precondition of criticising it responsibly. **Named evidence.** The description it offers — industrialisation, urbanisation, mass education, occupational differentiation, bureaucratic capacity, scientific rationality and expanding markets — is largely accurate; what converts it into a theory is the addition of a linear sequence of comparable stages, a convergence claim, a traditional/modern contrast, a diffusion mechanism and a warrant for expert direction. **Analysis.** Each standard critique then lands on a specific assumption: dependency denies the stage and convergence claims by holding that underdevelopment can be produced through unequal incorporation rather than inherited; post-development denies the warrant for expert direction by asking by what authority whole societies are defined as deficient; cultural and Gandhian critiques deny the traditional/modern contrast by refusing to concede that one conception of the good life is simply superior; Marxist criticism denies convergence and diffusion by locating the question in the ownership of production and the appropriation of the surplus; and the Ambedkarite objection asks whether modern institutions dismantle graded hierarchy or merely reorganise it into new occupations and offices. **Qualification.** The verdict must therefore be two-sided: the structural transformations are real and cannot be denied, but they count as progress only where they enlarge equal capability, democratic agency and ecological security — so modernisation is answerable to a standard it does not itself supply.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ The critique proves too much. If no comparable sequence exists and no institutional convergence occurs, then cross-society comparison is impossible and every society becomes its own standard — which makes the very idea of development, and of this clause, incoherent.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The denial is of a **single universal route**, not of comparability as such. Some capacities — the ability to be nourished, to be schooled, to avoid premature death, to appear in public without shame, to participate in decisions — are comparable across societies without presupposing that they must be reached by one path or embodied in one set of institutions. The correct position is **plural development under universal constraints**: many routes, but a shared floor of dignity, freedom from severe deprivation, equal civic standing and ecological responsibility.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The universal floor is easier to state than to specify at its boundary. Beyond the clearest capacities the list becomes contested, and the specification is itself vulnerable to the post-development objection about who is doing the specifying — which is why the floor must be defended by public reasoning rather than asserted by expertise.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Presenting modernisation as neutral inevitability — "as countries develop, they naturally become modern". That sentence contains three of the five assumptions and concedes the argument before it starts.
2. Denying the structural transformations altogether. Industrialisation, urbanisation and mass schooling are real, and denying them reads as ideology rather than analysis.
3. Naming the critiques without mapping them to assumptions. "Dependency and post-development criticise modernisation" is a sentence about the literature; naming which assumption each denies is a sentence about the argument.
4. Omitting the Ambedkarite question in an Indian answer. It is the objection that makes an Indian illustration analytical rather than decorative.

#### Mini recap

Separate the description from the theory. The description — industrialisation, urbanisation, schooling, differentiation, bureaucracy, scientific rationality, markets — is real. The theory adds five assumptions: linear sequence, convergence, traditional/modern contrast, diffusion, expert direction. Four hostile objections attack them: dependency, post-development, cultural/Gandhian, Marxist — and the Ambedkarite question asks whether hierarchy is dismantled or merely reorganised. Verdict: real transformations, no neutral law; institutions count as progress only when they enlarge equal capability, democratic agency and ecological security. The middle path is plural development under universal constraints.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 5
  modernisation -> separate the DESCRIPTION from the FIVE ASSUMPTIONS
      -> MECHANISM: sequence | convergence | traditional-modern | diffusion |
         expert direction
      -> CONSEQUENCE: each critique attacks a named assumption; the
         Ambedkarite question tests the whole package
      -> TRAP: neutral inevitability; blanket denial; unmapped critiques
      -> ANSWER USE: concede the transformations, deny the law, close on
         plural development under universal constraints
```

### SESSION 6 — HUMAN DEVELOPMENT: THE SHIFT FROM THE WEALTH OF THE ECONOMY TO THE LIVES OF PEOPLE

#### The picture first

```text
   THE HUMAN-DEVELOPMENT SHIFT -- WHAT MOVED, AND WHAT DID NOT
   ===========================================================

   BEFORE                                AFTER
   THE UNIT OF ACCOUNT IS THE ECONOMY    THE UNIT OF ACCOUNT IS THE PERSON
   "how much is being produced?"         "how long, how informed and how
                                          materially secure are the lives
                                          being lived?"
        |                                        |
        v                                        v
   +----------------------+          +--------------------------------+
   | OUTPUT PER HEAD      |  ====>   | LONGEVITY   | KNOWLEDGE |      |
   | one number, one      |  SHIFT   | a decent MATERIAL STANDARD     |
   | space, one silence   |          | three dimensions, one composite|
   +----------------------+          +--------------------------------+
                                                  |
   ASSOCIATED WITH: MAHBUB UL HAQ and the UNDP    |
   human-development framework                    |
                                                  v
   WHAT THE SHIFT ACHIEVES -- STATE THIS PRECISELY
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | IT BREAKS THE IDENTITY BETWEEN INCOME AND WELL-BEING. TWO          |
   | SOCIETIES WITH THE SAME OUTPUT PER HEAD CAN DIFFER SHARPLY IN HOW  |
   | LONG PEOPLE LIVE AND WHAT THEY KNOW -- AND THAT DIFFERENCE IS NOW  |
   | VISIBLE INSTEAD OF INVISIBLE.                                      |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                                  |
                                                  v
   WHAT THE SHIFT DOES NOT ACHIEVE -- FOUR RESIDUAL CONCEALMENTS
     DISTRIBUTION ...... a national composite can improve while a
                         disadvantaged group inside it stands still
     FREEDOM ........... political liberty and voice are not among the
                         dimensions
     GENDER AND
     DISABILITY ........ conversion differences between persons remain
                         invisible inside an aggregate
     ECOLOGICAL
     DEPENDENCE ........ the stock drawn down to produce the improvement
                         does not appear
                                                  |
                                                  v
   THE POSITION OF HUMAN DEVELOPMENT ON THE LADDER
     R1 GROWTH -> R2 ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT -> [R3 HUMAN DEVELOPMENT] ->
     R4 CAPABILITY -> R5 SOCIAL PROGRESS
   IT IS A DECISIVE ADVANCE ON R1 AND R2 AND AN INCOMPLETE ANTICIPATION
   OF R4: IT COUNTS ACHIEVED STATES (functionings), NOT REAL OPPORTUNITIES
   (capabilities), AND IT COUNTS PEOPLE AS BENEFICIARIES, NOT AS AGENTS.
```

*Caption: the human-development shift shown as a change in the unit of account, the identity it breaks, the four things it still conceals, and its exact position on the ladder — a decisive advance on output measures and an incomplete anticipation of the capability approach.*

#### In plain words

The human-development framework did something deceptively simple: it changed what the numbers are *about*. Before the shift, the standard summary of how a society was doing described the economy — how much it produced, how fast that was rising, how it was distributed between sectors. After the shift, the standard summary described **people** — how long they live, what they know, and whether they command enough resources for a decent standard of living.

That sounds like a technical adjustment. It is not. It is a change of subject, and it has an immediate consequence: two societies producing the same amount per head can now be seen to differ enormously in how long their people live and how much schooling they get. Before the shift, that difference had nowhere to appear. ⚠️ Breaking the identity between income and well-being is the achievement, and it is worth stating in exactly those words, because it is what the framework is *for*.

But it is important to be equally precise about what did not change. A national composite is still an average, so a group that stands still while the average rises remains invisible. Political freedom is not among the dimensions, so a society can score well while its people cannot speak. Differences between persons in how much a given resource yields — a disabled person needs more to reach the same functioning — are averaged away. And the ecological stock drawn down to produce the improvement does not appear anywhere. Those four residual concealments are exactly the openings through which the capability approach enters in the next session.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The doctrine.** The human-development approach, associated with **Mahbub ul Haq** and the **UNDP** framework, shifts the focus from the wealth of the economy to the lives of people, especially **longevity, knowledge and a decent material standard**.

✅ **Its strength, stated exactly.** It **breaks the identity between income and well-being**. This is the sentence to write; vaguer formulations — "it is a broader measure" — do not earn the mark.

✅ **Its limitation, stated exactly.** Composite national measures can **obscure distribution, freedom, gender, disability and ecological dependence**.

⚠️ **Its position on the ladder.** Human development is R3: a decisive advance on growth and economic development, and an **incomplete anticipation** of the capability approach at R4. The incompleteness has two named parts. First, it counts **achieved states** — being alive at a given age, having attended school — which are *functionings*, not the *real opportunities* that capability measures. Second, it counts people as **beneficiaries** whose outcomes are recorded, not as **agents** whose reasoning and choices are part of the good being measured.

⚠️ **Why the incompleteness matters for the 2022 owned part.** The 2022 stem asks whether economic development *on its own* leads to human development and social progress. A script that treats human development as the terminus has conceded half the question, because human development is itself only a rung — and the stem names social progress separately for exactly that reason.

✅ **The discipline point.** ❌ No index score, rank or figure is required. The examinable content is the change of informational space and the four residual concealments, not any published number.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Mahbub ul Haq and the UNDP human-development framework** ✅ — the historical instance of a deliberate change in the unit of account, from the economy to the person.
- **The identity-breaking formulation** ✅ — "it breaks the identity between income and well-being" is the precise statement of the achievement and is directly usable in the 2022 answer.
- **The four residual concealments** ✅ — distribution, freedom, gender and disability, ecological dependence; each of the four is the doorway to a later session, and naming them shows the framework's limits without dismissing it.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The human-development framework did not merely widen the measure; it changed the unit of account from the economy to the person, and its achievement is precisely that it breaks the identity between income and well-being — while leaving distribution, freedom, gender, disability and ecological dependence still concealed inside the aggregate.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**unit of account** · **wealth of the economy against the lives of people** · **longevity, knowledge, decent material standard** · **breaks the identity between income and well-being** · **composite national measure** · **within-group invisibility** · **achieved state against real opportunity** · **beneficiary rather than agent**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The human-development approach is best understood not as a wider indicator but as a change in the unit of account, and its philosophical achievement is that it breaks the identity between income and well-being. **Named evidence.** The framework associated with Mahbub ul Haq and the UNDP replaces output per head with longevity, knowledge and a decent material standard, so that two societies producing the same amount per head can be seen to differ in how long people live and what they are able to learn — a difference that has no place to appear inside an output measure. **Analysis.** The consequence is decisive for the modal stems in this clause, because it shows that the inference from economic development to improved lives is contingent rather than automatic: what a society produces and what its members are able to be and do are recorded in different informational spaces, and the relation between them depends on how resources are converted. **Qualification.** The advance is nevertheless incomplete in four specified respects — a national composite conceals within-group inequality, political freedom is not among the dimensions, differences in conversion arising from gender and disability are averaged away, and the ecological stock drawn down to finance the improvement does not appear — and it counts achieved states rather than real opportunities, which is precisely the gap the capability approach was constructed to close.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ If composite national measures conceal distribution, freedom, gender, disability and ecology, then the human-development framework is barely an improvement on output measures: it has simply substituted one misleading average for another.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The substitution is not neutral, because the two averages are averages of different things. An output average can rise with no improvement in any life at all; a longevity-and-schooling average cannot, since it is composed of achievements in lives. The framework therefore guarantees a floor of relevance that an output measure does not, and its concealments are corrigible — disaggregation by group, by gender and by region is available within the same framework, whereas the output measure cannot be disaggregated into well-being at all.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Disaggregation improves the diagnosis without closing the deeper gap, because the framework still records **achieved functionings** rather than the **opportunities** persons actually had. A society that produces long, schooled lives by compulsion would score identically to one that produces them by expanding choice, and no amount of disaggregation distinguishes the two.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Treating human development as the final standard. It is R3; the owned 2022 part names social progress separately precisely because the two are not the same.
2. Reducing the framework to a comparison of two indices. The examinable content is the change of unit and the identity it breaks, not a ranking of measures.
3. Quoting an index value or rank. ❌ No figure is required, and an unsourced one is a discipline failure.
4. Presenting human development and capability as the same approach. Human development counts achieved states and treats people as beneficiaries; capability counts real opportunities and treats people as agents.

#### Mini recap

The shift changed the unit of account from the economy to the person: longevity, knowledge, a decent material standard. Its achievement is that it breaks the identity between income and well-being. Its limitation is that composite national measures conceal distribution, freedom, gender, disability and ecological dependence. Its position is R3 — decisive over R1 and R2, incomplete against R4, because it records achieved functionings rather than real opportunities and counts people as beneficiaries rather than agents. No figure is required anywhere.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 6
  human development -> a change in the UNIT OF ACCOUNT, not a wider index
      -> MECHANISM: longevity + knowledge + decent material standard
      -> CONSEQUENCE: the identity between income and well-being is broken
      -> TRAP: treating R3 as the terminus; quoting an index; merging it
         with capability
      -> ANSWER USE: state the identity-breaking achievement, then name the
         four residual concealments as the doorway to capability
```

---

### SESSION 7 — SEN I: FUNCTIONING, CAPABILITY, RESOURCE, UTILITY, AGENCY

#### The picture first

```text
   THE FIVE TERMS, AND THE CONVERSION THAT JOINS THEM
   ==================================================

   RESOURCE                CONVERSION FACTORS          CAPABILITY
   a means whose value  --> personal (body, age,   -->  the REAL OPPORTUNITY
   depends on what it       impairment, metabolism)     to achieve alternative
   can be turned into       environmental (climate,     FUNCTIONINGS
                            terrain, infrastructure)         |
   e.g. a bicycle,          social (norms, safety,           | CHOICE
   a grain ration,          discrimination, public           v
   an income                provision)                  FUNCTIONING
                                                        an ACHIEVED being or
                                                        doing -- being
                                                        nourished, moving
                                                        freely, participating

   THE DIAGRAM READ IN ONE SENTENCE
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | EQUAL RESOURCES YIELD UNEQUAL FREEDOM, BECAUSE BODIES,             |
   | ENVIRONMENTS AND SOCIAL NORMS DIFFER. THAT IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT.  |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+

   THE FIFTH AND SIXTH TERMS -- THE TWO THAT SCRIPTS DROP
   +--------------+----------------------------------------------------+
   | UTILITY      | satisfaction. IT ADAPTS DOWNWARD under deprivation, |
   |              | so it can certify the deprivation it measures.      |
   +--------------+----------------------------------------------------+
   | AGENCY       | the ability to pursue goals ONE HAS REASON TO       |
   |              | VALUE, INCLUDING GOALS BEYOND ONE'S OWN WELFARE.    |
   |              | A person can be well-fed and still not be an agent. |
   +--------------+----------------------------------------------------+

   THE THREE SPACES, SIDE BY SIDE -- THIS IS THE EXAMINABLE CONTRAST
   +-------------+-------------------+-----------------------------------+
   | SPACE       | WHAT IS COUNTED   | WHY IT FAILS AS THE FINAL SPACE   |
   +-------------+-------------------+-----------------------------------+
   | RESOURCES   | goods and income  | ignores CONVERSION differences    |
   | (means)     | held              | between persons                   |
   +-------------+-------------------+-----------------------------------+
   | UTILITY     | reported          | ADAPTS to deprivation; respects   |
   | (mental     | satisfaction      | the report and endorses the       |
   | states)     |                   | injustice                         |
   +-------------+-------------------+-----------------------------------+
   | CAPABILITY  | REAL OPPORTUNITY  | <-- SEN'S ANSWER. It counts what  |
   | (freedom)   | to achieve valued | persons can ACTUALLY do, and it   |
   |             | beings and doings | leaves the choosing to them       |
   +-------------+-------------------+-----------------------------------+

   THE DISTINCTION THAT DECIDES MOST MCQS AND HALF THE MARKS
     FASTING and STARVING produce the SAME FUNCTIONING (undernourishment)
     and RADICALLY DIFFERENT CAPABILITY SETS. THE FIRST HAS THE OPTION TO
     EAT AND DECLINES IT; THE SECOND HAS NO OPTION AT ALL.
     -> CAPABILITY IS THE OPPORTUNITY SET, NOT THE OUTCOME CHOSEN FROM IT.
```

*Caption: the five terms arranged as a single conversion process, the two terms scripts routinely drop, the three candidate informational spaces with the reason each of the first two fails, and the fasting-and-starving contrast that fixes the capability/functioning distinction in one line.*

#### In plain words

Sen's argument is often summarised as "development is freedom", which is true and almost useless, because it does not say what kind of freedom or how it is measured. The working content is a set of five terms and one relation between them, and once you have them the rest of the clause becomes tractable.

Start with the relation. A resource — a bicycle, a grain ration, an income — is not valuable in itself. It is valuable because of what a person can turn it into. How much a person can turn it into depends on facts about her body, her environment and her society: whether she can ride, whether the road exists, whether it is safe for her to be on it, whether norms permit her to travel alone. Those are the **conversion factors**, and they are the reason equal resources yield unequal freedom.

What the conversion produces is a **capability** — a real opportunity to achieve some set of beings and doings. What she then does with the opportunity is a **functioning** — an achieved being or doing. ⚠️ The distinction between the two is where most marks are won and lost, and the clean way to fix it is the contrast between fasting and starving. Both are undernourished; both have the same functioning. One has the option to eat and declines it; the other has no option at all. If you measure only the functioning you cannot tell them apart, and any theory that cannot tell them apart is not a theory of freedom.

Then two further terms. **Utility** is satisfaction, and its problem is that it moves: people who have been deprived for a long time adjust what they expect and report contentment. A metric that trusts the report will certify the deprivation. **Agency** is the ability to pursue goals one has reason to value — *including goals that are not about one's own welfare*. That clause is the one scripts always drop, and it is what separates a person who is well-provided-for from a person who is free.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **Sen's account of development.** Development is the expansion of **substantive freedom** and the removal of unfreedoms. Resources matter through their **conversion** into capabilities.

✅ **The five core terms.** A **functioning** is an achieved being or doing — being nourished, moving freely, participating. A **capability** is the real opportunity to achieve alternative functionings. A **resource** is a means whose value depends on conversion conditions. **Utility** is satisfaction, which may adapt downward under deprivation. **Agency** is the ability to pursue goals one has reason to value, **including goals beyond personal welfare**.

✅ **Conversion factors.** Personal — body, age, impairment, metabolism. Environmental — climate, terrain, infrastructure. Social — norms, safety, discrimination, public provision. ⚠️ Naming the three kinds converts "conversion varies" from an assertion into an analysis.

✅ **The first two steps of the argument.** (1) Equal resources yield unequal freedom because bodies, environments and social norms differ. (2) Utility can adapt to deprivation.

⚠️ **The presupposition.** Persons are **agents**, not passive recipients of welfare. Everything distinctive in Sen's framework — the emphasis on public reasoning, the refusal to fix a final list, the insistence on participation as constitutive — follows from this single presupposition.

⚠️ **The capability/functioning discipline.** Capability is the **opportunity set**; functioning is the **element chosen from it**. Promoting compulsory functionings — requiring people to eat, to work, to be schooled in one way — secures functionings at the cost of the freedom that made them valuable. This is why capability, not functioning, is the space of evaluation.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Sen, *Development as Freedom*** ✅ — development as the expansion of substantive freedom and the removal of unfreedoms; cited by author and title only, with no page or wording asserted.
- **Sen, *Commodities and Capabilities*** ✅ — the treatment of goods as means whose value depends on conversion into what persons can be and do.
- **The three kinds of conversion factor** ✅ — personal, environmental, social; the analytical device that lets an answer explain *why* equal resources produce unequal freedom instead of merely asserting it.
- **The fasting/starving contrast** ⚠️ — the standard reconstruction that fixes the capability/functioning distinction in a single line and is worth deploying in any capability answer.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Income is a means of development and capability is a measure of what the means enable: equal resources yield unequal freedom because bodies, environments and social norms differ, so the developmental question is never how much is held but what can actually be achieved with it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**substantive freedom** · **functioning** · **capability** · **conversion factors — personal, environmental, social** · **utility and adaptive preference** · **agency** · **goals beyond personal welfare** · **opportunity set against chosen element** · **removal of unfreedoms**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Sen's reconstruction of development as the expansion of substantive freedom is not a slogan but a specification of the informational space in which development is to be judged, and its working content is the distinction between resources, functionings and capabilities. **Named evidence.** In *Development as Freedom* and *Commodities and Capabilities* the argument runs through conversion: a resource is a means whose value depends on what a person can turn it into, and how much she can turn it into depends on personal factors such as body and impairment, environmental factors such as terrain and infrastructure, and social factors such as norms, safety and public provision. **Analysis.** It follows that equal resources yield unequal freedom, so a resource metric misdescribes equality; and since utility adapts downward under long deprivation, a satisfaction metric can certify the very deprivation it records. Capability escapes both because it counts the real opportunity to achieve alternative functionings rather than the goods held or the contentment reported — which is why fasting and starving, identical as functionings, are radically different as capability sets. **Qualification.** The framework presupposes that persons are agents rather than recipients, and this both grounds its refusal to promote compulsory functionings and exposes it to the objection that selecting and measuring relevant capabilities is contested — an objection Sen answers by referring the selection to public reasoning rather than to expertise, which relocates the difficulty without eliminating it.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ Capability is unusable in practice. Opportunity sets are counterfactual — they concern what a person *could* have done — and counterfactuals cannot be observed, so any actual measurement will fall back on achieved functionings and the distinction will collapse in application.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The objection describes a difficulty of estimation, not a failure of the concept, and the difficulty is routinely managed. Conversion factors are observable: impairment, distance, infrastructure, discriminatory norms and the availability of public provision can all be recorded, and together they license inferences about opportunity that go well beyond the observed outcome. Two people with the same nutritional status but different access to food are not indistinguishable to any competent inquiry.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Estimation nevertheless requires a **selection** of the capabilities that matter, and that selection is contested. Sen refers it to public reasoning; but public reasoning conducted under unequal power and adapted expectations may select badly, which is exactly the pressure point Nussbaum's threshold is designed to relieve (Session 9).

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Reducing Sen to "income plus health and education". That is the human-development framework, not the capability approach, and the reduction discards conversion, agency and the opportunity/outcome distinction in one stroke.
2. Confusing capability with achieved functioning. Capability is the opportunity set; the functioning is what was chosen from it, and the fasting/starving contrast exists to keep them apart.
3. Dropping "including goals beyond personal welfare" from the definition of agency. Without that clause agency collapses into self-interested choice and the argument for participation loses its constitutive half.
4. Treating conversion as a single vague idea. Naming the three kinds — personal, environmental, social — is what turns the point into an analysis.

#### Mini recap

Five terms: resource, conversion factor, capability, functioning, utility — with agency added as the sixth and most frequently dropped. Resources are means; their value depends on conversion; conversion varies personally, environmentally and socially; so equal resources yield unequal freedom. Utility adapts downward under deprivation and can certify it. Capability is the opportunity set; functioning is what is chosen from it; fasting and starving separate them in one line. The presupposition is that persons are agents, not recipients — and everything distinctive in the approach follows from it.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 7
  resources -> CONVERSION FACTORS (personal | environmental | social)
      -> MECHANISM: capability = the real opportunity set; functioning =
         the element chosen from it
      -> CONSEQUENCE: resource metrics misdescribe equality; utility metrics
         certify deprivation
      -> TRAP: Sen reduced to income plus health and education; capability
         confused with functioning; agency stripped of non-welfare goals
      -> ANSWER USE: open with conversion, fix the distinction with
         fasting/starving, close on agency
```

### SESSION 8 — SEN II: FREEDOM AS BOTH END AND MEANS, AND THE ADAPTIVE-PREFERENCE PROBLEM

#### The picture first

```text
   THE FIVE-STEP ARGUMENT, AND THE TWO ROLES OF FREEDOM
   ====================================================

   STEP 1 . equal resources yield UNEQUAL FREEDOM -- bodies, environments
            and social norms differ
              |
   STEP 2 . UTILITY CAN ADAPT to deprivation; the long-deprived may report
            satisfaction
              |
   STEP 3 . therefore development must assess REAL OPPORTUNITIES, not only
            goods held or happiness reported
              |
   STEP 4 . the instrumental freedoms REINFORCE ONE ANOTHER
              |
   STEP 5 . FREEDOM IS BOTH THE END AND A MEANS OF DEVELOPMENT
              |
              v
   THE FIVE INSTRUMENTAL FREEDOMS -- AND THE POINT OF THE CIRCLE
   +--------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | POLITICAL FREEDOMS | voice, dissent, scrutiny, the ability to      |
   |                    | force a public response                      |
   +--------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | ECONOMIC           | access to resources, exchange, work,          |
   | FACILITIES         | credit and markets                           |
   +--------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | SOCIAL             | schooling, health care, and the public        |
   | OPPORTUNITIES      | provision that raises conversion              |
   +--------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | TRANSPARENCY       | the ability to deal with one another under    |
   | GUARANTEES         | disclosure -- the condition of trust          |
   +--------------------+----------------------------------------------+
   | PROTECTIVE         | the safety net that prevents an ordinary      |
   | SECURITY           | shock from becoming destitution               |
   +--------------------+----------------------------------------------+
        THEY ARE NOT A LIST. EACH RAISES THE VALUE OF THE OTHERS:
        schooling raises the usefulness of political voice; voice forces
        the public provision that produces schooling; transparency makes
        economic facility usable; protective security is what allows a
        person to exercise any of the others without ruin.

   THE TWO ROLES OF FREEDOM -- WRITE BOTH OR LOSE THE ARGUMENT
   +---------------------------+--------------------------------------+
   | CONSTITUTIVE (the END)     | INSTRUMENTAL (the MEANS)             |
   | being able to participate,  | participation improves information,  |
   | to be schooled, to appear   | accountability and the accuracy of   |
   | in public without shame IS  | public decisions, and therefore      |
   | PART OF A DEVELOPED LIFE    | PRODUCES other freedoms              |
   +---------------------------+--------------------------------------+
   AN ANSWER THAT GIVES ONLY THE INSTRUMENTAL HALF HAS MADE FREEDOM
   NEGOTIABLE: IT CAN THEN BE TRADED AWAY WHENEVER THE OUTPUT IS LARGER
   WITHOUT IT. THAT IS THE AUTHORITARIAN-DEVELOPMENT ARGUMENT, AND THE
   CONSTITUTIVE HALF IS WHAT DEFEATS IT.

   ADAPTIVE PREFERENCE -- THE MECHANISM, DRAWN
     long deprivation -> expectations adjust downward -> the deprivation
     is no longer EXPERIENCED as a loss -> satisfaction is REPORTED ->
     a welfare metric RECORDS CONTENTMENT -> the injustice is CERTIFIED
     BY THE VERY RESPECT SHOWN TO THE REPORT
   THE DANGER IN THE REPLY: overriding what people say is PATERNALISM.
   THE DISCIPLINE: use capability (opportunity), not the analyst's
   judgment of what people should want.
```

*Caption: the five-step argument set out in order, the five instrumental freedoms shown as a reinforcing circle rather than a list, the two roles of freedom with the reason both must be written, and the adaptive-preference mechanism drawn step by step together with the paternalism danger in its reply.*

#### In plain words

Session 7 gave the terms; this session gives the argument they were built for, and it has a shape worth memorising because it survives compression to five lines in an examination.

It begins where Session 7 ended: equal resources yield unequal freedom. It adds the awkward fact that asking people how satisfied they are will not repair the problem, because satisfaction adapts. From those two premises it concludes that development must be assessed in the space of **real opportunities**. Then it adds the claim that does the political work: the different freedoms are not independent goods to be traded against one another but **mutually reinforcing** — schooling makes political voice more effective, voice forces the public provision that produces schooling, transparency makes economic opportunity usable, and protective security is what allows a person to risk exercising any of the others.

⚠️ And then the conclusion that most scripts get half-right: freedom is both the **end** and a **means** of development. Writing only the instrumental half — "freedom is useful because it improves outcomes" — is a serious error, and not a subtle one. It makes freedom negotiable: if it is valuable only for what it produces, then it may be suspended whenever someone argues that output would be higher without it. That is precisely the authoritarian-development argument, and the constitutive claim — that being able to participate, to be schooled, to appear in public without shame is *part of what a developed life is* — is what defeats it.

The adaptive-preference point deserves care, because it is easy to state in a way that is condescending. The claim is **not** that deprived people are mistaken about their own lives. It is that a metric built on reported satisfaction will systematically under-record deprivation that has lasted long enough for expectations to adjust — and that the correct response is to change the metric to opportunity, not to override the report with the analyst's own view of what people ought to want.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The full five-step argument.** (1) Equal resources yield unequal freedom because bodies, environments and social norms differ. (2) Utility can adapt to deprivation. (3) Development must therefore assess real opportunities, not only goods or happiness. (4) Political freedoms, economic facilities, social opportunities, transparency guarantees and protective security **reinforce one another**. (5) Freedom is both the **end** and a **means** of development.

✅ **The five instrumental freedoms.** Political freedoms; economic facilities; social opportunities; transparency guarantees; protective security. ⚠️ Their significance is the **interlocking**, not the enumeration: an answer that lists them without stating that each raises the value of the others has recorded the name and lost the argument.

✅ **The two roles of freedom.** **Constitutive** — being able to participate, to be schooled, to move and to appear in public without shame is part of a developed life. **Instrumental** — those same freedoms improve information, accountability and the quality of public decisions, and so produce further freedoms.

⚠️ **Why both roles are compulsory.** The purely instrumental defence makes freedom tradeable and therefore supplies no protection when a stem proposes suspending it for the sake of faster development. The constitutive claim is what makes the trade unavailable in principle.

✅ **Adaptive preference, stated as a methodological claim.** Satisfaction reports adjust downward under long deprivation, so subjective welfare is an unreliable metric of development.

⚠️ **The paternalism danger in the reply.** Overriding reported preference without argument substitutes the analyst's judgment for the person's. The disciplined response is to shift the **evaluative space** to opportunity rather than to correct the person's stated wants — capability protects the option, and leaves the choosing where it belongs.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Sen, *Development as Freedom*** ✅ — freedom as both the end and the principal means of development; the five instrumental freedoms and their mutual reinforcement.
- **Sen's adaptive-preference argument** ✅ — the decisive objection to satisfaction-based metrics, usable in any measurement paragraph and in the 2022 and 2023 owned parts.
- **The constitutive/instrumental pair** ⚠️ — the reconstruction that defeats the authoritarian-development argument, and the single most useful move in any stem that asks whether participation slows development down.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Freedom is both the end and the means of development: instrumentally it improves information, accountability and the quality of public decisions, and constitutively it is part of what a developed life consists in — which is why it cannot be traded away for a larger output without changing the subject.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**substantive freedom** · **five instrumental freedoms** · **mutual reinforcement** · **constitutive role** · **instrumental role** · **adaptive preferences** · **unreliable metric** · **paternalism danger** · **shift the evaluative space, not the person's wants** · **removal of unfreedoms**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** On Sen's account freedom occupies two roles in development simultaneously — it is the end for which development is undertaken and the principal means by which it proceeds — and an answer that supplies only the second has made freedom negotiable. **Named evidence.** *Development as Freedom* sets out five instrumental freedoms — political freedoms, economic facilities, social opportunities, transparency guarantees and protective security — and the argument's force lies in their mutual reinforcement: schooling raises the effectiveness of political voice, voice compels the public provision that produces schooling, transparency makes economic facility usable, and protective security is what allows a person to exercise any of the others without risking ruin. **Analysis.** Because the freedoms interlock, development cannot be modelled as a trade-off in which liberty is exchanged for output; and because utility adapts downward under long deprivation, the apparent contentment of the deprived cannot be used as evidence that the trade was acceptable to them. The proper conclusion is that the evaluative space must be real opportunity, which registers what a person could do rather than what she reports or possesses. **Qualification.** The adaptive-preference argument must be handled carefully, since the claim is methodological rather than condescending: it says that a satisfaction metric will under-record long-standing deprivation, not that deprived persons are mistaken about their own lives — and the disciplined response is to change the metric to capability rather than to override what people say, because overriding it substitutes the analyst's judgment for the agent's and reintroduces the paternalism the framework exists to avoid.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ The adaptive-preference argument is self-undermining. If the preferences of the deprived are unreliable, then their participation in public reasoning is unreliable too — and Sen's own refusal to fix a capability list, which refers the selection to public reasoning, rests on exactly that participation.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The objection conflates two things. Adaptation affects reported **satisfaction with a condition**; it does not disable **reasoning about alternatives** once alternatives are visible and voice is available. The remedy is therefore internal to the framework: expand information, protect dissent and secure the conditions under which preferences can be revised — which is exactly what the instrumental freedoms do, and it is why transparency and political freedom appear among them rather than as optional extras.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The remedy presupposes that the conditions of open public reasoning can be secured before the selection is made, and in societies where deprivation and unequal power are entrenched the presupposition may fail. Nussbaum's threshold is one response to that failure; whether a specified list can be defended without the paternalism it was meant to avoid is the live dispute of the next session.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Writing only the instrumental role of freedom. It makes freedom tradeable and hands the authoritarian-development argument its premise.
2. Listing the five instrumental freedoms without stating that they reinforce one another. The interlocking *is* the argument; the list is only its index.
3. Using adaptive preference condescendingly — "the poor do not know what is good for them". The claim is about what a metric can record, not about what people understand.
4. Answering the paternalism objection by asserting that experts should override preferences. The disciplined reply changes the evaluative space to opportunity and leaves the choosing to the agent.

#### Mini recap

Five steps: unequal conversion, adaptive utility, therefore opportunity as the space, interlocking freedoms, freedom as both end and means. Five instrumental freedoms — political, economic, social, transparency, protective security — significant because each raises the value of the others. Two roles: constitutive and instrumental; write both, because the instrumental alone makes freedom tradeable. Adaptive preference is a methodological claim about metrics, not a claim about the competence of the deprived, and the disciplined reply shifts the evaluative space to capability rather than overriding what people say.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 8
  unequal conversion + adaptive utility -> assess REAL OPPORTUNITIES
      -> MECHANISM: five instrumental freedoms, mutually reinforcing
      -> CONSEQUENCE: freedom is END and MEANS; the trade-off model fails
      -> TRAP: instrumental half only; list without interlocking;
         condescending use of adaptive preference
      -> ANSWER USE: write both roles, deploy the interlocking against any
         "participation delays development" stem, close on capability as the
         space that avoids paternalism
```

---

### SESSION 9 — NUSSBAUM: THE DIGNITY THRESHOLD AND THE SEN–NUSSBAUM DISPUTE

#### The picture first

```text
   TWO WAYS TO USE ONE FRAMEWORK
   =============================

                        THE CAPABILITY APPROACH
                                 |
              +------------------+------------------+
              |                                     |
        SEN'S USE                             NUSSBAUM'S USE
   an EVALUATIVE SPACE plus a            a DETERMINATE ACCOUNT of central
   METHOD: identify the relevant         human capabilities plus a
   capabilities through PUBLIC           THRESHOLD each person must reach
   REASONING, case by case               for a life COMPATIBLE WITH DIGNITY
              |                                     |
              v                                     v
   +------------------------+          +------------------------------+
   | STRENGTH               |          | STRENGTH                     |
   | avoids PATERNALISTIC   |          | resists societies that       |
   | CLOSURE; the list is   |          | publicly reason INSIDE       |
   | not fixed by theory    |          | OPPRESSIVE ADAPTIVE          |
   |                        |          | PREFERENCES                  |
   +------------------------+          +------------------------------+
   | WEAKNESS               |          | WEAKNESS                     |
   | public reasoning under |          | a universal list can IMPOSE  |
   | unequal power may      |          | ONE CONCEPTION of a good life|
   | select badly           |          |                              |
   +------------------------+          +------------------------------+
              |                                     |
              +------------------+------------------+
                                 v
   THE REPLY THAT MAKES THE THRESHOLD DEFENSIBLE -- MEMORISE THIS
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | CAPABILITIES PROTECT OPPORTUNITIES, NOT COMPULSORY FUNCTIONINGS.   |
   | THE STATE SECURES THE OPTION; THE PERSON DECIDES WHETHER TO TAKE   |
   | IT. A THRESHOLD OF OPTIONS IS THEREFORE NOT A PRESCRIPTION OF A    |
   | LIFE -- AND POLITICAL SPECIFICATION REMAINS OPEN TO DEMOCRATIC     |
   | INTERPRETATION.                                                    |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                 |
                                 v
   THE THRESHOLD AS A POLITICAL-CONSTITUTIONAL MINIMUM
     NOT a maximising target -- it does not say "more is always better"
     NOT an aggregate -- a gain for many does not offset a person left
        below the line
     BUT a FLOOR PER PERSON -- justice requires EACH person to reach it,
        which is why the claim is one of JUSTICE and not of benevolence
                                 |
                                 v
   WHERE THIS BITES IN THIS CLAUSE
     - tribal and community stems: a group's authority over its members
       is bounded by each member's threshold (exit, dissent, gender
       justice)
     - welfare stems: "they are satisfied with what they have" cannot
       defeat a threshold claim
     - trade-off stems: a threshold cannot be traded against aggregate
       output, because it is not an aggregate quantity
```

*Caption: one framework used two ways — Sen's evaluative space and public reasoning against Nussbaum's specified list and dignity threshold — each with its strength and its weakness; the reply that makes a threshold defensible; the threshold's character as a per-person floor rather than a target; and the three places in this clause where it decides the answer.*

#### In plain words

It is tempting to treat Nussbaum as "Sen plus a list", and that reading loses the point. The disagreement is not about how many capabilities there are. It is about what the framework is **for**.

Sen uses the capability approach as an evaluative space together with a method: it tells you where to look — at real opportunities rather than at goods or satisfaction — and it refers the question of *which* opportunities matter to public reasoning in the society concerned. The attraction is obvious: no theorist gets to decide in advance what a good life contains, and the framework stays open to plural conceptions.

Nussbaum presses a hard question at that openness. What happens when the public reasoning is itself conducted inside oppressive expectations — when the people whose capabilities are being denied have adjusted to the denial, and when the society's reasoning reproduces the hierarchy rather than examining it? Referring the selection to that reasoning does not protect anyone. So she supplies a determinate account of central human capabilities and argues that **justice** — not charity, not policy preference — requires each person to reach a **threshold** compatible with dignity.

⚠️ The obvious objection is that a universal list imposes one conception of the good life. The reply is the sentence to memorise: **capabilities protect opportunities, not compulsory functionings**. The threshold secures the option to be nourished, to be schooled, to move, to associate, to participate; it does not require anyone to exercise it in a prescribed way. And the political specification of the list remains open to democratic interpretation rather than fixed once by a theorist.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **Nussbaum's position.** She supplies a **more determinate account of central human capabilities** and argues that **justice requires each person to reach a threshold compatible with dignity**.

✅ **The Sen–Nussbaum distinction, stated exactly.** Sen **resists a single final canonical list** and stresses **public reasoning**. Nussbaum **defends a specified list and threshold** as a **constitutional-political minimum**.

✅ **The objection.** A universal list may impose one conception of life.

✅ **The reply.** Capabilities protect **opportunities**, not compulsory functionings; political specification should remain **open to democratic interpretation**.

⚠️ **The comparative assessment to write.** Sen **avoids paternalistic closure**; Nussbaum **better resists societies that publicly reason within oppressive adaptive preferences**. Each strength is the other's weakness, and stating the pair is what converts a comparison into an adjudication.

⚠️ **Three properties of a threshold that decide answers.** It is a **floor per person**, not a maximising target — it does not say more is always better. It is **not aggregative** — a gain for many does not offset one person left below it. And because it attaches to persons, it converts the claim from benevolence into **justice**.

⚠️ **Why this matters for the owned tribal part.** A threshold bounds a community's authority over its own members: recognition of community rights is compatible with insisting on exit, dissent and gender justice inside the community, because each member's threshold is not the community's to trade. This is developed in Session 20 and it is the mechanism by which cultural recognition avoids collapsing into the freezing of internal hierarchy.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Nussbaum, *Creating Capabilities*** ✅ — justice requires each person to reach a threshold of central capabilities compatible with dignity.
- **Nussbaum, *Women and Human Development*** ✅ — the argument that adaptive preference under entrenched hierarchy is precisely what a threshold is needed to resist.
- **The Sen–Nussbaum dispute** ✅ — evaluative space and public reasoning against a specified list and constitutional minimum; the paradigm live dispute of this owner, and the one to flag as unresolved rather than to settle.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> A threshold of central capabilities is not a prescription of how to live but a floor beneath which no person may be left, because capabilities protect opportunities rather than compelling functionings — and a floor that attaches to each person cannot be discharged by an average that rises.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**central human capabilities** · **threshold compatible with dignity** · **constitutional-political minimum** · **evaluative space and public reasoning** · **paternalistic closure** · **oppressive adaptive preferences** · **opportunities not compulsory functionings** · **floor per person** · **non-aggregative** · **justice rather than benevolence**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The disagreement between Sen and Nussbaum is not about the number of capabilities but about what the capability framework is for — an evaluative space whose content is settled by public reasoning, or a determinate constitutional minimum owed to each person as a matter of justice. **Named evidence.** Sen, in *Development as Freedom*, resists fixing a single canonical list and refers the selection of relevant capabilities to public reasoning; Nussbaum, in *Creating Capabilities* and *Women and Human Development*, specifies central human capabilities and argues that justice requires each person to reach a threshold compatible with dignity. **Analysis.** Each position is strongest exactly where the other is weakest. Sen's openness avoids paternalistic closure, but it offers little protection where public reasoning is itself conducted inside entrenched hierarchy and adapted expectations; Nussbaum's threshold resists that failure, but invites the charge that a universal list imposes one conception of the good life — a charge answered by the observation that capabilities protect opportunities rather than compelling functionings, and that the political specification of the list remains open to democratic interpretation. **Qualification.** The threshold's decisive properties are that it is a floor per person and not an aggregate, so a rise in the average cannot discharge it and a gain for many cannot offset one person left below it; but who fixes the threshold, and by what procedure, remains genuinely unsettled, and an answer that declares the dispute closed in either direction has overclaimed.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ A threshold is either trivial or tyrannical. Pitched low enough to command agreement it adds nothing to a basic-needs standard; pitched high enough to do real work it encodes a particular, culturally specific idea of a flourishing life and imposes it as a constitutional requirement.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The dilemma has a middle, and the middle is occupied by the **opportunity** form of the claim. A threshold of *options* — to be adequately nourished, to be schooled, to move, to associate, to participate, to be free of avoidable humiliation — is substantially more demanding than a basic-needs floor, because it requires the conversion conditions and not merely the goods; and it is substantially less intrusive than a prescription of functionings, because it never requires that any option be exercised. That is precisely why the list is stated as capabilities and not as required achievements.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The middle position still requires someone to fix where the floor sits, and the specification is not derivable from the framework itself. Nussbaum locates it in constitutional-political practice open to democratic interpretation, which is a reasonable answer and not a complete one — the procedure by which a society sets the floor is exposed to the same inequalities of power that motivated the threshold in the first place.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Describing Nussbaum as "Sen with a list". The disagreement is about the role of the framework, not about enumeration, and the list is a consequence rather than the thesis.
2. Omitting the reply. A script that states the imposition objection without "capabilities protect opportunities, not compulsory functionings" has left Nussbaum undefended and will read as a survey.
3. Treating the threshold as a target to be maximised. It is a per-person floor; a rising average does not discharge it and an aggregate gain does not offset a person below it.
4. Declaring the dispute settled. ❓ It is live, and flagging it as live is itself the mark of a controlled answer.

#### Mini recap

One framework, two uses. Sen: evaluative space plus public reasoning; avoids paternalistic closure; vulnerable where public reasoning is conducted inside oppression. Nussbaum: specified central capabilities plus a dignity threshold as a constitutional-political minimum; resists oppressive adaptive preference; vulnerable to the charge of imposing one conception of life. The reply: capabilities protect opportunities, not compulsory functionings, and specification stays open to democratic interpretation. The threshold is a per-person, non-aggregative floor, which makes it a claim of justice. It bounds a community's authority over its members — the mechanism that keeps cultural recognition from freezing internal hierarchy.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 9
  one capability framework -> TWO USES: evaluative space + public reasoning
      (Sen) | specified list + dignity threshold (Nussbaum)
      -> MECHANISM: capabilities protect OPPORTUNITIES, not compulsory
         functionings; specification stays democratically open
      -> CONSEQUENCE: a per-person, non-aggregative floor = a claim of justice
      -> TRAP: "Sen with a list"; objection without reply; threshold as target;
         declaring the dispute settled
      -> ANSWER USE: pair each strength with the other's weakness, then use
         the threshold to bound community authority in tribal stems
```

### SESSION 10 — DEPENDENCY AND POST-DEVELOPMENT: PRODUCED UNDERDEVELOPMENT AND THE AUTHORITY TO DIAGNOSE

#### The picture first

   (2) CONVERGENCE              contrast at (3)

   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+

                          diagnosis does not by itself supply schools,
                          clinics or security
