**Necessity.** Yes, in a **threshold** form: adequate material capabilities are necessary, because severe deprivation restricts health, education and agency. ❓ "Economic development" is too broad to be logically necessary in every conceivable community.
**Sufficiency.** No. Wealth can coexist with exclusion, authoritarianism, gender hierarchy and ecological destruction — one clean counter-case, stated at the level of criteria, with no society or figure named.
**Verdict.** An important **enabling condition**, neither conceptually identical with nor sufficient for social progress.
**10+5 split.** Ten for the two tests; five for the graded verdict plus the point that identity and enabling are different relations.
❓ **Residual.** Where the threshold sits is not settled and varies with conversion conditions.

### J. Redistribution, recognition and the agency ladder

**Two demands.** Redistribution of resources; recognition of equal status. **Two corruptions:** provision without respect is paternalism; standing without means is symbolic.
**Four-way grid.** Resources + recognition = progress on this axis. Resources without recognition = paternalism. Recognition without resources = symbolic equality. Neither = compounded disadvantage.
**Inherited-power test.** Growth redistributes income at best; in India caste, gender and tribal status show that it does not redistribute standing. Used as criteria; doctrines routed.
**Independent, not sequential.** Humiliation does not wait for prosperity, and provision without standing entrenches the relation it was meant to relieve.
**Agency ladder.** Information (one-way disclosure is not voice) → consultation (may be ignored) → participation (elites can monopolise) → co-decision (needs clear responsibility and rights safeguards) → **empowerment** (durable capability and institutional power; not inferable from attendance).
**Two reasons for participation.** Instrumental — information and accountability. Constitutive — voice is part of a developed life; this is the half that makes it non-tradeable.
**Objection and reply.** Delay and elite capture are objections to badly designed participation; capture is a failure of **inclusion**, and deciding without the affected merely relocates it. Remedy: inclusive design, reasons, representation, review.
**The test.** Could the decision have been different? If not, it was at most consultation.

### K. Technology and skill education (2019, 2023)

**Technology.** Capacity without ends. Four optimist/critical pairs — communication and surveillance; medicine and unequal access; productivity and displacement; transparency and reproduced bias. One point four times: **control, access and purpose** fix the direction. **Four conversion conditions:** ethical purposes, fair access, accountability, human control. **Concession:** technology reshapes which moral problems arise without supplying the criterion for settling them. It is the non-inevitability argument applied. ❌ Name no technology, platform, company or episode.
**Skill education.** Standard = capability and agency, not employment. **Positive argument:** capability needs effective means → skills improve conversion → participation raises contribution and bargaining power. **Four limits:** frustrated capability without decent work; narrow employability displacing civic and critical education; unequal access reproducing hierarchy; obsolescence, so the durable capability is the capacity to re-learn. **Two mirror errors:** sufficiency, and mere instrumentality — the second defeated by dignity of labour. **Test:** bargaining power, not usefulness to an employer. **Position:** livelihood, adaptability, dignity of labour and critical citizenship together. ❌ No employment figures, scheme names or outlays.

### L. Gandhian social development (2020)

**Doctrine.** Welfare and moral elevation of all (*sarvodaya*) through self-rule (*swarāj*), non-violence, decentralisation, dignity of labour, trusteeship, restraint of wants and village-centred production.
**Five-step argument.** Unlimited wants create dependence, competition and violence → centralised industrial power separates production from need and local control → means shape ends → self-rule requires local economic and moral capacity → therefore decentralised, need-oriented, ecologically restrained development.
**Four presuppositions.** Moral self-limitation is politically relevant; small-scale association can sustain participation; work has value beyond income; change can proceed through non-violent conversion and constructive action.
**Three objections and replies.** Romantic village → defensible only with equal rights and anti-domination; **Ambedkar's criticism is indispensable**. Technological conservatism → read it as a test of scale, control and need, not a ban on machines. Trusteeship as voluntary paternalism → it morally delegitimises absolute ownership, but institutional mechanisms are needed for accountability.
**Two comparisons.** Against industrial modernisation → selective adoption by capability, decentralised control, ecological cost and non-domination. Against Ambedkar on the village → local self-government is progressive only where constitutional rights, mobility and anti-caste safeguards transform local power.
**Restraint objection.** It targets multiplied and manufactured wants, not need — the same asymmetry as the ecological argument. ❓ The doctrine supplies a direction, not a line.
**Verdict.** Survives in a **conditioned** form: a standing test of scale and control inside guarantees it does not itself supply.

### M. Development and tribal values (2025)

**Reframe.** Not modernity against tradition, but five questions about authority and distribution: who defines benefit; who bears cost and risk; is land only a commodity or also identity and relation; whose knowledge counts; are consent and self-government real.
**Description discipline.** Tribal communities are internally diverse; avoid romantic generalisation. Many such conflicts involve community control of resources, ecological dependence, customary institutions, intergenerational continuity and collective identity.
**Seven-step model.** Equal citizenship and cultural recognition → capabilities gained **and destroyed** → informed inclusive participation → individual and community rights without freezing culture → fair distribution of benefits, burdens **and decision-power** → exit, dissent and gender justice preserved → reversible and sustainable alternatives under grave uncertainty.
**Four tests, required jointly.** (i) Both sides of the capability ledger, including the community's own. (ii) Participation in the **decision**, not merely the compensation. (iii) A precautionary presumption against irreversible ecological and cultural loss. (iv) No shifting of adjustment burdens onto those least able to bear them.
**Indian recognitions, classified.** PESA, **1996** — enacted statute, Gram Sabha role in Scheduled Areas. Forest Rights Act, **2006** — enacted rights-recognition statute, individual and community forest rights. *Orissa Mining Corporation v. Ministry of Environment & Forest*, **2013** — Supreme Court judgment involving Gram Sabha consideration. Articles **48A** and **51A(g)** — non-justiciable. **Enactment is not notification; notification is not implementation; implementation is not capability expansion; a judgment is not legislation; a non-justiciable provision establishes commitment, not outcome.**
**Verdict.** Reconcilable only where communities are **agents** of development rather than obstacles or objects.

### N. Ecological political philosophy

**Three orientations.** Anthropocentric — humans only; permits destruction where no human interest is engaged. Biocentric — every individual living thing; cannot adjudicate between organisms or protect a system as such. Ecocentric — ecological wholes; risks subordinating individuals.
**Discriminating question.** Would it be wrong to destroy a wilderness no human will ever see, use or know of? Anthropocentric: no. Biocentric and ecocentric: yes, differently.
**Practical warning.** ❌ Do not adopt strong ecocentrism casually — it licenses coercive limits for systemic goods. Defensible position: strong, non-instrumental anthropocentrism supplemented by duties of stewardship, stated and defended.
**Naess (1973).** Shallow fights pollution and depletion for the health and affluence of the developed world; deep questions the framework and affirms intrinsic value in non-human life. **Depth of questioning, not degree of concern.**
**Productivism and degrowth.** Productivism is shared by industrial socialism and capitalism. Degrowth: finite sinks and stocks → efficiency offset by expanded volume → post-threshold flattening in affluent societies → permanent aggregate growth neither feasible nor necessary for them → planned reduction in throughput around sufficiency, care, shorter working time and equality → burden on high-consuming economies and classes. **Asymmetry: contraction and convergence.** ❓ Weakest link: that a non-growing economy can maintain employment, services and stability.
**Capability bridge.** Beyond the level at which output secures capability, further growth has no automatic developmental claim — Sen reaches the same conclusion by a non-ecological route.
**Intergenerational justice.** Duties of justice, not benevolence; the Brundtland Report (1987) builds it into the concept — ❌ paraphrase, never quote. Three problems: non-identity, motivation and reciprocity, discounting and pure time preference. **Specification:** preserve options, avoid irreversibility; uncertainty then argues for precaution.
**Environmentalism of the poor.** Guha and Martinez-Alier (1997): conflict over livelihood, not post-material taste — so environment and development are the same question for such communities and "environment versus development" is a framing available only to those whose subsistence is not at stake. The real question is **distributive**. Objection: romanticism. Reply: it describes the structure of conflict, and secure rights convert dependence into stewardship.
**Eco-authoritarianism and just transition.** Four replies in ascending strength — epistemic, compliance, record, and the decisive distributive one. **Verdict:** the ecological case strengthens the argument for participation.

### O. Inter-school debates, criticisms and the sixteen traps

**Five debates.** Growth model against Sen — output against substantive opportunity; consumer or factor against agent; distribution after growth against conversion and freedom as central; democracy possibly instrumental against instrumental and constitutive. Sen against Nussbaum — evaluative space and public reasoning against a list and dignity threshold. Gandhi against industrial modernisation — scale, productivity and mastery against the test of scale, control and need; selective adoption. Gandhi against Ambedkar on the village — school of self-rule against institutionalised caste exclusion. Modernisation against post-development — institutional transformation against imposed standards; plural development under universal constraints.
**Six criticisms with replies and residuals.** Capability is hard to measure → evaluative richness beats false precision; residual: policy needs indicators. Universal capabilities are paternalistic → protect opportunity, permit plural functionings; residual: who fixes the threshold. Participation romanticises communities → include dissent, rights and review; residual: elite capture. Sustainability blocks poor societies' growth → affluent consumption and unequal burdens are the target; residual: transition costs. Gandhianism is anti-modern → a critique of scale, wants and domination; residual: complex infrastructure. Social progress is value-relative → freedom from severe deprivation and humiliation have cross-cultural force; residual: reasonable disagreement persists.
**The sixteen traps.** (1) Growth, development, human development and progress are not synonyms. (2) Never say economic development is simply "necessary and sufficient" — state the material-threshold nuance. (3) Sen is not income plus welfare; capability is real freedom and agency matters. (4) Capabilities are not achieved functionings. (5) A human-development composite is not a complete theory of progress. (6) Technology does not create ethical progress. (7) Skill education is not an employment statistic. (8) Do not romanticise tribal culture or the village. (9) Enactment of PESA or the Forest Rights Act is not implementation or success. (10) A scheme, statute or judgment is not philosophical proof. (11) An indicator is not a theory of development — state its informational space and blind spot. (12) Modernisation is not neutral convergence or an inevitable sequence. (13) Not every social change is progress. (14) Consultation is not empowerment. (15) Do not use current social or economic figures; none is required. (16) Do not import full liberalism, Marxism or Ambedkar's caste theory — use the development criterion and route the doctrine.
**Directive decoder.** *Explain* → definition, components, distinction from the neighbouring term, example. *Discuss* → conception, rival, objection, reply, verdict. *Evaluate* → name the standard, apply, concede the counter-case, verdict. *Necessary/sufficient?* → test each separately, state which holds and why. *Do you agree that…?* → qualified position at the outset, reasons, counter-case, restated position. *Is it possible to reconcile X with Y?* → what each requires, the collision, the conditions under which both are satisfied. *Does X lead to Y?* → mechanism, defeater, verdict on the conditional.
**PYQ ledger, 6 of 112.** 2019 Q2(c) 15 — technology and ethical standards. 2020 Q2(a) 20 — the Gandhian conception, stated and examined. 2022 Q3(a) 20 — economic development on its own. 2023 Q2(c) 15 — skill education. 2024 Q3(c) 10+5 — necessary, sufficient, both or neither. 2025 Q3(c) 15 — reconciliation with tribal values. Distribution: 2018: 0, 2019: 1, 2020: 1, 2021: 0, 2022: 1, 2023: 1, 2024: 1, 2025: 1.

```text
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
FIVE SILENCES OF GROWTH -- EACH ONE IS A PARAGRAPH
  DISTRIBUTION ...... who receives the additional product?
  WORK QUALITY ...... under what conditions is it produced?
  DISPLACEMENT ...... who loses land, livelihood or habitat for it?
  POLITICAL VOICE ... who decided, and could anyone have refused?
  ECOLOGICAL COST ... what stock or sink was drawn down, and is it reversible?
        |
        v
IS THE CONVERSION THESIS UNFALSIFIABLE? -- NO, AND SAY WHY
  THE CONVERSION CONDITIONS ARE NAMEABLE IN ADVANCE: distributive rules | work
  quality and security | protection against displacement without consent |
  effective political voice | ecological limits on irreversibility.
  A CASE WHERE ALL FIVE OBTAIN AND LIVES DO NOT IMPROVE WOULD COUNT AGAINST IT.
  RESIDUAL PROBLEM: the five are weighted differently by the liberal, Marxist
  and Gandhian lenses, so the thesis inherits the disagreement it organises.
        |
        v
VERDICT FORMULA -> "GROWTH BECOMES PROGRESS ONLY THROUGH JUST CONVERSION INTO
  CAPABILITY, DIGNITY AND SUSTAINABLE INSTITUTIONS; THE CONVERSION CONDITIONS,
  NOT THE OUTPUT FIGURE, ARE WHERE THE NORMATIVE QUESTION LIES."


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 6/22: Modernisation -- five assumptions, five objections
WHAT IT DESCRIBES (real; denying it reads as ideology)
  industrialisation | urbanisation | mass education | occupational
  differentiation | bureaucratic capacity | scientific rationality |
  expanding markets -- a transition from "traditional" to "modern"
        |
        v
THE FIVE ASSUMPTIONS THAT MAKE IT A THEORY
  1 LINEAR SEQUENCE ...... societies move through broadly comparable stages
  2 CONVERGENCE .......... institutional differences DIMINISH as capacity grows
  3 TRADITIONAL/MODERN ... inherited authority and local production YIELD to
    CONTRAST             rational, mobile, differentiated institutions
  4 DIFFUSION ............ capital, knowledge and technology TRANSMIT
                           development
  5 EXPERT DIRECTION ..... planners identify obstacles and ACCELERATE transition
        |
        v
FIVE OBJECTIONS, EACH MAPPED TO WHAT IT DENIES
  +----------------+------------------------------------+------------------+
  | CRITIQUE       | WHAT IT CLAIMS                     | HITS ASSUMPTION  |
  +----------------+------------------------------------+------------------+
  | DEPENDENCY     | underdevelopment can be PRODUCED   | (1) and (2)      |
  |                | through unequal incorporation      |                  |
  +----------------+------------------------------------+------------------+
  | POST-          | by what AUTHORITY is a society     | (5)              |
  | DEVELOPMENT    | defined as DEFICIENT?              |                  |
  +----------------+------------------------------------+------------------+
  | CULTURAL /     | rejects HOMOGENISATION of the      | (3)              |
  | GANDHIAN       | good life                          |                  |
  +----------------+------------------------------------+------------------+
  | MARXIST        | who OWNS production and            | (2) and (4)      |
  |                | APPROPRIATES the gains?            |                  |
  +----------------+------------------------------------+------------------+
  | AMBEDKARITE    | does modernisation DISMANTLE       | the whole        |
  | (scripts omit  | graded hierarchy or merely         | package          |
  |  this one)     | REORGANISE it?                     |                  |
  +----------------+------------------------------------+------------------+
        |
        v
COUNTER-OBJECTION -> if no comparable sequence exists, CROSS-SOCIETAL
  COMPARISON becomes impossible and "development" collapses.
REPLY -> what is denied is a SINGLE UNIVERSAL ROUTE, not comparability. Being
  nourished, schooled, safe from premature death and able to participate are
  comparable WITHOUT one path. RESIDUAL: the floor's boundary is contested.
        |
        v
VERDICT -> MODERNISATION NAMES REAL STRUCTURAL TRANSFORMATIONS BUT IS NOT A
  NEUTRAL OR INEVITABLE LAW. ITS INSTITUTIONS COUNT AS PROGRESS ONLY WHEN THEY
  ENLARGE EQUAL CAPABILITY, DEMOCRATIC AGENCY AND ECOLOGICAL SECURITY.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 7/22: Human development -- Haq, the UNDP framework and its limits
THE SHIFT: A CHANGE IN THE **UNIT OF ACCOUNT**, NOT A WIDER INDEX
  BEFORE -> the unit is THE ECONOMY: "how much is being produced?"
  AFTER  -> the unit is THE PERSON: "how long, how informed and how materially
            secure are the lives being lived?"
  DIMENSIONS: LONGEVITY | KNOWLEDGE | A DECENT MATERIAL STANDARD
  ASSOCIATED WITH: MAHBUB UL HAQ and the UNDP human-development framework
        |
        v
THE ACHIEVEMENT -- WRITE IT IN THESE WORDS
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | IT BREAKS THE IDENTITY BETWEEN INCOME AND WELL-BEING. TWO SOCIETIES   |
  | WITH THE SAME OUTPUT PER HEAD CAN DIFFER SHARPLY IN HOW LONG PEOPLE   |
  | LIVE AND WHAT THEY KNOW -- AND THAT DIFFERENCE IS NOW VISIBLE.        |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
FOUR RESIDUAL CONCEALMENTS
  DISTRIBUTION ........... a composite rises while a group inside it stands still
  FREEDOM ................ political liberty and voice are NOT among the
                           dimensions
  GENDER AND DISABILITY .. conversion differences between persons are averaged
                           away
  ECOLOGICAL DEPENDENCE .. the stock drawn down to finance the gain never appears
        |
        v
POSITION ON THE LADDER -> R3. DECISIVE OVER R1 AND R2; INCOMPLETE AGAINST R4
  IN TWO NAMED RESPECTS:
    (a) it records ACHIEVED STATES (functionings), not REAL OPPORTUNITIES
        (capabilities)
    (b) it counts people as BENEFICIARIES whose outcomes are noted, not as
        AGENTS whose reasoning is part of the good measured
  CONSEQUENCE FOR 2022: the stem names SOCIAL PROGRESS SEPARATELY because human
  development is itself only a rung. Treating R3 as the terminus concedes half
  the question.
  DISCIPLINE: NO INDEX SCORE, RANK OR FIGURE IS REQUIRED ANYWHERE.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/22: Sen I -- five terms and three kinds of conversion factor
THE CHAIN
  RESOURCE ----> CONVERSION FACTORS ----> CAPABILITY ----(choice)----> FUNCTIONING
  a means whose   PERSONAL: body, age,     the REAL                    an ACHIEVED
  value depends   impairment, metabolism   OPPORTUNITY to              being or
  on what it can  ENVIRONMENTAL: climate,  achieve                     doing --
  be turned into  terrain, infrastructure  ALTERNATIVE                 nourished,
                  SOCIAL: norms, safety,   FUNCTIONINGS                moving,
                  discrimination, public                               participating
                  provision
        |
        v
THE WHOLE ARGUMENT IN ONE SENTENCE
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | EQUAL RESOURCES YIELD UNEQUAL FREEDOM, BECAUSE BODIES, ENVIRONMENTS   |
  | AND SOCIAL NORMS DIFFER.                                              |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
THE TWO TERMS SCRIPTS DROP
  UTILITY .... satisfaction. IT ADAPTS DOWNWARD under deprivation, so it can
               CERTIFY the deprivation it measures.
  AGENCY ..... the ability to pursue goals ONE HAS REASON TO VALUE, INCLUDING
               GOALS BEYOND ONE'S OWN WELFARE. A person can be well-fed and
               still not be an agent. DROPPING THE NON-WELFARE CLAUSE COLLAPSES
               AGENCY INTO SELF-INTERESTED CHOICE.
        |
        v
THREE CANDIDATE SPACES, AND WHY TWO FAIL
  RESOURCES (means) ....... ignores CONVERSION differences between persons
  UTILITY (mental states) . ADAPTS to deprivation; respects the report and
                            endorses the injustice
  CAPABILITY (freedom) .... SEN'S ANSWER: counts what persons can ACTUALLY do
                            and leaves the CHOOSING to them
        |
        v
THE DISTINCTION THAT DECIDES MOST QUESTIONS
  FASTING and STARVING = SAME FUNCTIONING (undernourished), RADICALLY DIFFERENT
  CAPABILITY SETS. The first has the option to eat and declines; the second has
  no option. CAPABILITY IS THE OPPORTUNITY SET, NOT THE OUTCOME CHOSEN FROM IT.
OBJECTION -> opportunity sets are COUNTERFACTUAL and cannot be observed.
REPLY -> conversion factors ARE observable -- impairment, distance,
  infrastructure, discriminatory norms, public provision -- and license
  inferences beyond the outcome. RESIDUAL: SELECTION of capabilities is contested.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 9/22: Sen II -- freedom as end and means; the five instrumental freedoms
THE FIVE-STEP ARGUMENT
  1. equal resources yield UNEQUAL FREEDOM
  2. UTILITY CAN ADAPT to deprivation
  3. therefore assess REAL OPPORTUNITIES, not goods held or happiness reported
  4. the instrumental freedoms REINFORCE ONE ANOTHER
  5. FREEDOM IS BOTH THE END AND A MEANS OF DEVELOPMENT
        |
        v
THE FIVE INSTRUMENTAL FREEDOMS -- AND WHY THEY ARE A CIRCLE, NOT A LIST
  POLITICAL FREEDOMS ....... voice, dissent, scrutiny, forcing a public response
  ECONOMIC FACILITIES ...... access to resources, exchange, work, credit, markets
  SOCIAL OPPORTUNITIES ..... schooling, health care, public provision that
                             RAISES CONVERSION
  TRANSPARENCY GUARANTEES .. dealing with one another under disclosure -- trust
  PROTECTIVE SECURITY ...... the net that stops an ordinary shock becoming
                             destitution
  EACH RAISES THE VALUE OF THE OTHERS: schooling raises the usefulness of voice;
  voice forces the provision that produces schooling; transparency makes economic
  facility usable; protective security is what lets a person exercise any of them.
        |
        v
THE TWO ROLES -- WRITE BOTH OR LOSE THE ARGUMENT
  +-----------------------------+-------------------------------------------+
  | CONSTITUTIVE (the END)      | INSTRUMENTAL (the MEANS)                  |
  | being able to participate,  | participation improves INFORMATION,       |
  | be schooled, appear in      | ACCOUNTABILITY and the accuracy of public |
  | public without shame IS     | decisions, and so PRODUCES other freedoms |
  | PART OF A DEVELOPED LIFE    |                                           |
  +-----------------------------+-------------------------------------------+
  INSTRUMENTAL HALF ALONE -> FREEDOM BECOMES TRADEABLE -> it may be suspended
  whenever output would be larger without it. THAT IS THE AUTHORITARIAN-
  DEVELOPMENT ARGUMENT, AND THE CONSTITUTIVE HALF IS WHAT DEFEATS IT.
        |
        v
CONSEQUENCE -> development CANNOT be modelled as a trade-off in which liberty is
  exchanged for output, because the freedoms are COMPLEMENTS, not competitors
  for a fixed budget.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 10/22: Adaptive preference and the paternalism discipline
THE MECHANISM, STEP BY STEP
  long deprivation
        -> EXPECTATIONS ADJUST DOWNWARD
        -> the deprivation is no longer EXPERIENCED as a loss
        -> SATISFACTION IS REPORTED
        -> a welfare metric RECORDS CONTENTMENT
        -> THE INJUSTICE IS CERTIFIED BY THE VERY RESPECT SHOWN TO THE REPORT
        |
        v
WHAT THE CLAIM IS -- AND IS NOT
  IT IS ....... a METHODOLOGICAL claim about what a satisfaction metric can
                record
  IT IS NOT ... a claim that the deprived DO NOT KNOW WHAT IS GOOD FOR THEM
  THE CONDESCENDING FORMULATION CHANGES THE THESIS RATHER THAN EXPRESSING IT.
        |
        v
THE DANGER IN THE REPLY -> overriding reported preference substitutes the
  ANALYST'S judgment for the PERSON'S. That is PATERNALISM -- the very thing the
  framework exists to avoid.
        |
        v
THE DISCIPLINE -> SHIFT THE EVALUATIVE SPACE TO OPPORTUNITY. Capability protects
  the OPTION and leaves the CHOOSING where it belongs. Do NOT correct the
  person's wants.
        |
        v
SELF-UNDERMINING OBJECTION -> if the preferences of the deprived are unreliable,
  so is their PUBLIC REASONING -- on which Sen's refusal to fix a list depends.
REPLY -> adaptation affects reported SATISFACTION WITH A CONDITION; it does not
  disable REASONING ABOUT ALTERNATIVES once alternatives are visible and voice is
  available. The remedy is INTERNAL: expand information, protect dissent, secure
  the conditions under which preferences can be REVISED -- which is exactly what
  transparency and political freedom do in Panel 9.
RESIDUAL -> where deprivation and unequal power are entrenched, those conditions
  may not obtain before the selection is made. THAT IS WHY NUSSBAUM'S THRESHOLD
  EXISTS (Panel 11).


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/22: Nussbaum -- the dignity threshold and the live dispute
ONE FRAMEWORK, TWO USES
  +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | SEN'S USE                      | NUSSBAUM'S USE                       |
  | an EVALUATIVE SPACE plus a     | a DETERMINATE ACCOUNT of CENTRAL     |
  | METHOD: identify relevant      | HUMAN CAPABILITIES plus a THRESHOLD  |
  | capabilities through PUBLIC    | each person must reach for a life    |
  | REASONING, case by case        | COMPATIBLE WITH DIGNITY              |
  +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | STRENGTH: avoids PATERNALISTIC | STRENGTH: resists societies that     |
  | CLOSURE                        | publicly reason INSIDE OPPRESSIVE    |
  |                                | ADAPTIVE PREFERENCES                 |
  +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | WEAKNESS: public reasoning     | WEAKNESS: a universal list can       |
  | under UNEQUAL POWER may select | IMPOSE ONE CONCEPTION of a good life |
  | badly                          |                                      |
  +--------------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  EACH IS STRONGEST EXACTLY WHERE THE OTHER IS WEAKEST. THE DISPUTE IS ABOUT
  WHAT THE FRAMEWORK IS **FOR**, NOT ABOUT HOW MANY CAPABILITIES THERE ARE.
        |
        v
THE REPLY THAT MAKES A THRESHOLD DEFENSIBLE -- MEMORISE
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | CAPABILITIES PROTECT OPPORTUNITIES, NOT COMPULSORY FUNCTIONINGS. THE  |
  | STATE SECURES THE OPTION; THE PERSON DECIDES WHETHER TO TAKE IT. AND  |
  | POLITICAL SPECIFICATION REMAINS OPEN TO DEMOCRATIC INTERPRETATION.    |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
THREE PROPERTIES OF A THRESHOLD THAT DECIDE ANSWERS
  NOT A MAXIMISING TARGET .. it does not say "more is always better"
  NOT AGGREGATIVE .......... a gain for many does not offset ONE person left
                             below the line
  A FLOOR PER PERSON ....... which is why the claim is one of JUSTICE and not
                             of benevolence
        |
        v
WHERE IT BITES IN THIS CLAUSE
  TRIBAL / COMMUNITY STEMS . a group's authority over its members is BOUNDED by
                             each member's threshold: EXIT, DISSENT, GENDER
                             JUSTICE
  WELFARE STEMS ............ "they are satisfied with what they have" cannot
                             defeat a threshold claim
  TRADE-OFF STEMS .......... a threshold cannot be traded against aggregate
                             output, because it is not an aggregate quantity
DILEMMA -> a threshold is either TRIVIAL (a basic-needs floor) or TYRANNICAL
  (one culture's flourishing). MIDDLE: a threshold of OPTIONS is more demanding
  than basic needs (it requires CONVERSION conditions) and less intrusive than
  prescribed functionings. RESIDUAL: WHO FIXES THE FLOOR remains unsettled.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/22: Dependency and post-development -- two attacks, one middle path
TARGET -> the modernisation claim that underdevelopment is an EARLIER STAGE on
  ONE UNIVERSAL PATH, curable by DIFFUSION and EXPERT DIRECTION.
        |
        +-------------------------------+
        |                               |
  DEPENDENCY                      POST-DEVELOPMENT
  an ECONOMIC-STRUCTURAL claim    an EPISTEMIC-POLITICAL claim
  "Underdevelopment can be        "By what AUTHORITY does an external expert
   PRODUCED through UNEQUAL        define a whole society as DEFICIENT and
   INCORPORATION into global       impose ONE MODEL OF MODERNITY as the cure?"
   economic relations, rather
   than being a mere earlier      -> attacks the WARRANT, not the economics
   stage."                        -> the question is WHO DIAGNOSES, on whose
                                     behalf
  ATTACKS assumptions (1),(2)     ATTACKS assumption (5), and behind it (3)
  REMEDY POINTS TO: terms of      REMEDY POINTS TO: who decides, and whose
  incorporation, ownership of     account of their own interests is
  gains                           authoritative
        |                               |
        +---------------+---------------+
                        v
SHARED OBJECTION -> romanticising local life IGNORES INTERNAL HIERARCHY,
  POVERTY and REAL DEMANDS for education, health and mobility.
SHARED REPLY -> the strongest critique rejects IMPOSED UNIFORMITY, not EVERY
  MATERIAL IMPROVEMENT. LOCAL VOICE MUST BE JOINED TO UNIVERSAL DIGNITY.
                        |
                        v
THE MIDDLE PATH -- THE POSITION TO DEFEND
  PLURAL DEVELOPMENT UNDER UNIVERSAL CONSTRAINTS
  MANY ROUTES | ONE FLOOR: dignity, freedom from severe deprivation, equal civic
  standing, ecological responsibility
        |
        v
WHAT EACH CRITIQUE CANNOT DO
  DEPENDENCY ......... can UNDERSTATE domestic institutional and political
                       factors; not a complete explanation of any case
  POST-DEVELOPMENT ... has NO POSITIVE PROGRAMME; refusing a diagnosis does not
                       supply schools, clinics or security
SELF-REFUTATION CHARGE -> post-development itself prescribes from outside.
REPLY -> the disciplined claim is PROCEDURAL: the community's own account is not
  to be DISPLACED WITHOUT ARGUMENT, and the BURDEN OF JUSTIFICATION falls on the
  party proposing intervention. RESIDUAL: WHO SPEAKS FOR THE COMMUNITY -- which
  is why the threshold (Panel 11) and exit/dissent (Panel 21) are needed.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 13/22: Sustainable development -- four bases, four specifications
THE FORMULA -> present improvement that does NOT DESTROY the ecological
  conditions and OPTIONS of future persons. Built into the concept by the
  BRUNDTLAND REPORT (1987). PARAPHRASE IT; NEVER REPRODUCE IT VERBATIM.
        |
        v
FOUR PHILOSOPHICAL BASES
  1 INTERGENERATIONAL JUSTICE ..... duties to those not yet born -- JUSTICE, not
                                    benevolence
  2 PRECAUTION UNDER IRREVERSIBLE
    RISK .......................... where the loss cannot be undone, UNCERTAINTY
                                    ARGUES FOR RESTRAINT, not for waiting
  3 LIMITS TO SUBSTITUTION ........ money cannot stand in for every ecological
                                    system; some stocks and sinks have NO
                                    SUBSTITUTE
  4 DUTIES TOWARD THE VULNERABLE .. plus the POSSIBLE INTRINSIC VALUE of
                                    non-human life
        |
        v
THE OBJECTION THAT MUST BE CONCEDED -> the concept is VAGUE and can legitimise a
  "GREEN" VERSION OF THE SAME GROWTH MODEL. "Sustainable" becomes an adjective
  attached to whatever was already planned.
        |
        v
THE REPLY -> SPECIFY FOUR THINGS OR THE WORD DOES NO WORK
  THRESHOLDS | DISTRIBUTION | PARTICIPATION | NON-SUBSTITUTABLE ECOLOGICAL LIMITS
        |
        v
OBJECTION -> restraint constrains exactly the WRONG SOCIETIES: those below a
  deprivation threshold are denied what the affluent already took.
REPLY -> the objection defeats a UNIFORM prescription and fails against the
  correct ASYMMETRIC one. DISTRIBUTION is one of the four specifications and is
  the one most often omitted.
RESIDUAL -> the asymmetry names a principle without settling TRANSITION COSTS:
  who finances expansion below the threshold, over what period, with what
  enforceable commitment.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/22: The five bounded evaluative lenses and the fence
USE AS CRITERIA. NEVER RE-EXPOUND THE DOCTRINE. THE FENCE IS EXAMINABLE.
  +--------------+------------------------+------------------------+---------+

  +--------------+------------------------+------------------------+---------+

  | WELFARE      | rights, public goods,  | coexist with UNEQUAL   | ONLY    |

  +--------------+------------------------+------------------------+---------+
  | MARXIST      | production, class      | growth can REPRODUCE   | COMPARE |
  |              | power, alienation,     | EXPLOITATION and       | ONLY    |
  |              | control of surplus     | commodity domination   |         |
  +--------------+------------------------+------------------------+---------+
  | HUMANISTIC / | each person as an END  | beneficiaries COUNTED  | FULL    |

  |              | freedoms               |                        | HERE    |
  +--------------+------------------------+------------------------+---------+
  | GANDHIAN     | need, non-violence,    | SCALE and MULTIPLIED   | DIRECT  |
  |              | local self-rule,       | WANTS can CENTRALISE   | OWNER   |
  |              | dignity of labour,     | DOMINATION             | (2020)  |
  |              | restraint              |                        |         |
  +--------------+------------------------+------------------------+---------+

        |

CONTROLLING SENTENCE -> THE MATRIX SUPPLIES EVALUATION CRITERIA, NOT FIVE
  COMPLETE IDEOLOGIES. IMPORTING THE FULL DOCTRINE OF ANY OF THEM BREACHES THE
  FENCE AND WILL BE READ AS AN OFF-TOPIC ANSWER.
        |
        v
HOW TO USE IT -> ONE LENS AS THE PRIMARY STANDARD, A SECOND AS THE CRITICAL
  COUNTERWEIGHT, THE REST ROUTED. TWO LENSES FULLY WORKED BEAT FIVE NAMED.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 15/22: Social progress -- weak teleology and four conceptions
WHAT KIND OF CLAIM IS "THIS IS PROGRESS"?
  WEAK TELEOLOGY .... directional AFTER the valued ends are stated  <- DEFEND
  STRONG TELEOLOGY .. history moves that way BY ITSELF              <- REJECT
  DOCTRINE -> SOCIAL PROGRESS is JUSTIFIED IMPROVEMENT in the quality of social
  relations, institutions and human lives, assessed through FREEDOM, EQUALITY,
  JUSTICE, DIGNITY, PARTICIPATION, SECURITY and SUSTAINABILITY.
        |
        v
THE ARGUMENT AGAINST INEVITABILITY -- FOUR STEPS
  1. TECHNOLOGY enlarges capacities for BOTH CARE AND DESTRUCTION
  2. INSTITUTIONS distribute its CONTROL and its RISKS
  3. MORAL LEARNING CAN BE REVERSED
  4. THEREFORE progress requires AGENCY, CONFLICT, INSTITUTIONS AND JUDGMENT
  HISTORICAL DEFEATER -> twentieth-century war, genocide and ecological crisis
  break any inference from KNOWLEDGE or TECHNOLOGY to MORAL IMPROVEMENT.
        |
        v
FOUR CONCEPTIONS -- EACH TRUE OF SOMETHING, NONE COMPLETE
  +-----------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | CONCEPTION      | STRENGTH                  | FAILURE                  |
  +-----------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | LINEAR /        | explains LEARNING and     | mistakes CAPACITY for    |
  | CUMULATIVE      | institutional ACCUMULATION| MORAL IMPROVEMENT, and   |
  |                 |                           | one route for a          |
  |                 |                           | universal sequence       |
  +-----------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | CYCLICAL        | resists COMPLACENT        | can become FATALISTIC    |
  |                 | inevitability             | and understate reform    |
  +-----------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | DIALECTICAL /   | explains CONFLICT and     | may impose a SINGLE      |
  | CONFLICTUAL     | DISCONTINUITY             | historical direction     |
  +-----------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | PLURAL /        | respects CULTURE,         | risks RELATIVISM and     |
  | NON-LINEAR      | CONTINGENCY,              | loss of common standards |
  |                 | REVERSIBILITY             |                          |
  +-----------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
        |
        v
CONTROLLING POSITION -> DIRECTIONAL ONLY AFTER THE VALUED ENDS ARE STATED, AND
  PLURAL ONLY WITHIN A UNIVERSAL FLOOR OF: DIGNITY | FREEDOM FROM SEVERE
  DEPRIVATION | EQUAL CIVIC STANDING | ECOLOGICAL RESPONSIBILITY.
RELATIVIST OBJECTION -> "improvement" reports the observer's preferences.
REPLY -> some criteria have CROSS-CULTURAL FORCE, and the clearest are NEGATIVE:
  no society's members ask to be starved or degraded. RESIDUAL: reasonable
  disagreement persists ABOVE the floor and about its BOUNDARY.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 16/22: The seven criteria and the modal test (2024)
SEVEN CRITERIA, EACH AS A QUESTION YOU CAN PUT TO A CASE
  FREEDOM ............. do people have REAL OPTIONS and VOICE?
  EQUALITY ............ are STATUS and OPPORTUNITY less structured by DOMINATION?
  JUSTICE ............. are BENEFITS, BURDENS and PROCEDURES defensible?
  DIGNITY ............. are persons protected from HUMILIATION and EXCLUSION?
  PARTICIPATION ....... do AFFECTED PEOPLE SHAPE DECISIONS?
  SUSTAINABILITY ...... are FUTURE and ECOLOGICAL conditions preserved?
  FRATERNITY /
  SOLIDARITY .......... can citizens relate as SOCIAL EQUALS?
  NO SINGLE AGGREGATE SETTLES ALL SEVEN. PUBLIC REASONING IS PART OF PROGRESS.
        |
        v
RUN THE TWO MODAL TESTS SEPARATELY -- THEY ARE INDEPENDENT
  NECESSARY  = the ABSENCE BLOCKS the result
  SUFFICIENT = the PRESENCE SECURES the result
  A CONDITION MAY BE EITHER, BOTH OR NEITHER. "IT IS VERY IMPORTANT" ANSWERS
  NEITHER AND SCORES ALMOST NOTHING.
        |
        v
TEST 1 -- IS ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT NECESSARY?
  FOR: severe deprivation restricts HEALTH, EDUCATION and AGENCY, and every
       other criterion presupposes persons CAPABLE OF ACTING
  PRECISION: "economic development" is TOO BROAD to be logically necessary in
       EVERY conceivable community
  EXAM-SAFE CLAIM: ADEQUATE MATERIAL CAPABILITIES ARE NECESSARY
  -> VERDICT: NECESSARY IN A **THRESHOLD** FORM. WRITE THE WORD "THRESHOLD".
        |
        v
TEST 2 -- IS IT SUFFICIENT?
  ANSWER: NO, decisively
  COUNTER-CASE: WEALTH CAN COEXIST WITH EXCLUSION, AUTHORITARIANISM, GENDER
       HIERARCHY AND ECOLOGICAL DESTRUCTION
  WHY IT IS AVAILABLE: progress is assessed across SEVEN criteria; improvement
       on the material dimension leaves SIX untouched
  -> VERDICT: NOT SUFFICIENT, IN NO FORM. ONE CLEAN COUNTER-CASE SUFFICES; NO
     SOCIETY OR FIGURE IS TO BE NAMED.
        |
        v
COMBINED VERDICT (answers the 2024 stem)
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT IS GENERALLY AN IMPORTANT **ENABLING CONDITION** |
  | BUT IS NEITHER CONCEPTUALLY IDENTICAL WITH NOR SUFFICIENT FOR SOCIAL  |
  | PROGRESS: NECESSARY ONLY IN A THRESHOLD FORM, SUFFICIENT IN NO FORM.  |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  10+5 SPLIT -> TEN for the two tests run separately; FIVE for the graded
  verdict plus the point that IDENTITY and ENABLING are DIFFERENT RELATIONS.
  RESIDUAL -> WHERE THE THRESHOLD SITS is not settled and varies with conversion
  conditions. Concede it before the examiner does.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/22: Redistribution, recognition and the agency ladder
TWO DEMANDS THAT CANNOT SUBSTITUTE FOR EACH OTHER
  REDISTRIBUTION of RESOURCES -- what people HAVE
  RECOGNITION of EQUAL STATUS -- how people are REGARDED
  WITHOUT RECOGNITION -> provision becomes PATERNALISM (goods to objects of
                         policy)
  WITHOUT RESOURCES ---> recognition becomes SYMBOLIC (standing that cannot be
                         acted on)
        |
        v
THE FOUR-WAY GRID -- CLASSIFY ANY CASE IN ONE LINE
  +-------------------+------------------------+------------------------+
  |                   | RECOGNITION PRESENT    | RECOGNITION ABSENT     |
  +-------------------+------------------------+------------------------+
  | RESOURCES PRESENT | PROGRESS on this axis  | PATERNALISM            |
  +-------------------+------------------------+------------------------+
  | RESOURCES ABSENT  | SYMBOLIC EQUALITY      | COMPOUNDED             |
  |                   |                        | DISADVANTAGE           |
  +-------------------+------------------------+------------------------+
  INHERITED-POWER TEST -> GROWTH REDISTRIBUTES INCOME AT BEST. IT DOES NOT
  AUTOMATICALLY ALTER INHERITED SOCIAL POWER. IN INDIA, CASTE, GENDER AND TRIBAL
  STATUS SHOW THIS. USED AS CRITERIA; THE DOCTRINES ARE ROUTED.
  THE TWO ARE INDEPENDENT, NOT SEQUENTIAL: humiliation does not wait for
  prosperity, and provision without standing ENTRENCHES the relation.
        |
        v
THE FIVE-RUNG AGENCY LADDER
  5 EMPOWERMENT .... DURABLE CAPABILITY and INSTITUTIONAL POWER to shape
                     options, resources and rules -- including the capacity to
                     DISSENT, EXIT, ORGANISE and DEMAND REASONS.
                     CANNOT BE INFERRED FROM ATTENDANCE OR NOMINAL REPRESENTATION
  4 CO-DECISION .... SHARED AUTHORITY over alternatives | needs clear
                     responsibility and rights safeguards
  3 PARTICIPATION .. CONTINUING ROLE in design, implementation, review | LOCAL
                     ELITES can monopolise representation
  2 CONSULTATION ... opportunity to EXPRESS preferences | may simply be IGNORED
  1 INFORMATION .... notice and intelligible reasons | ONE-WAY DISCLOSURE IS
                     NOT VOICE
        |
        v
TWO REASONS PARTICIPATION MATTERS -- WRITE BOTH
  INSTRUMENTAL: better INFORMATION and ACCOUNTABILITY
  CONSTITUTIVE: HAVING A VOICE IS ITSELF PART OF A DEVELOPED HUMAN LIFE
                <- the half that makes participation NON-TRADEABLE
OBJECTION -> consultation DELAYS urgent projects and lets LOCAL ELITES VETO
  wider welfare.
REPLY -> INCLUSIVE DESIGN | REASONS | REPRESENTATION | REVIEW. Capture is a
  FAILURE OF INCLUSION -- an argument for MORE participation, not less, since
  deciding without the affected RELOCATES the capture.
THE TEST -> WHO WAS IN THE ROOM? WHO COULD HAVE SAID NO? WHAT ALTERNATIVES WERE
  ON THE TABLE? COULD THE DECISION HAVE BEEN DIFFERENT? IF NOT -- IT WAS AT MOST
  CONSULTATION. RESIDUAL: the genuinely URGENT and IRREVERSIBLE decision, where
  post-hoc review cannot restore what was foreclosed.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 18/22: Technology and ethical standards (2019 Q2(c), 15)
THE STEM IS A CAUSAL CLAIM -> SUPPLY A MECHANISM AND A DEFEATER, OR IT IS
  ASSERTION.
CORE PROPOSITION -> TECHNOLOGY CHANGES CAPACITIES AND SOCIAL RELATIONS. IT DOES
  NOT SUPPLY ITS OWN ENDS.
        |
        v
THE SYMMETRY -- EVERY CAPACITY CUTS BOTH WAYS IN THE SAME RESPECT
  +--------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
  | OPTIMIST CASE                  | CRITICAL CASE                           |
  +--------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
  | communication can WIDEN        | surveillance and manipulation can       |
  | KNOWLEDGE AND SYMPATHY         | INTENSIFY DOMINATION                    |
  +--------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
  | medicine REDUCES SUFFERING     | ACCESS can remain UNEQUAL               |
  +--------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
  | productivity can REDUCE        | automation can DISPLACE and DESKILL     |
  | DRUDGERY                       |                                         |
  +--------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
  | transparency tools can AID     | information systems can REPRODUCE BIAS  |
  | ACCOUNTABILITY                 |                                         |
  +--------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
  THESE ARE NOT A "BALANCED VIEW". THEY ARE ONE POINT MADE FOUR TIMES: THE
  DIRECTION OF THE EFFECT IS FIXED BY **WHO CONTROLS**, **WHO CAN ACCESS**, AND
  **TO WHAT END** -- NONE OF WHICH THE TECHNOLOGY SUPPLIES.
        |
        v
FOUR CONVERSION CONDITIONS -> ETHICAL PURPOSES | FAIR ACCESS | ACCOUNTABILITY |
  HUMAN CONTROL
VERDICT -> TECHNICAL PROGRESS BECOMES SOCIAL PROGRESS ONLY WHEN GOVERNED BY
  THOSE FOUR.
        |
        v
THE CONCESSION THAT LIFTS THE ANSWER -> THE RELATION IS NOT NULL. TECHNOLOGY
  RESHAPES **WHICH** MORAL PROBLEMS ARISE -- new occasions for choice, new
  possible harms, new meaningful duties. WHAT IT DOES NOT DO IS SUPPLY THE
  CRITERION BY WHICH ANY OF THEM IS SETTLED.
BUILT-IN-TENDENCY OBJECTION -> a capacity requiring CENTRALISED CONTROL to
  operate will produce centralisation whoever owns it.
REPLY -> ABSORB IT: the claim is that technologies are NOT SELF-JUSTIFYING, so a
  built-in tendency is itself an object of assessment BEFORE ADOPTION -- which is
  what "human control" among the four conditions means.
RESIDUAL -> assessment before adoption is hard: effects are often unknown until
  deployment is wide, and by then arrangements are costly to reverse.
DISCIPLINE -> NAME NO TECHNOLOGY, PLATFORM, COMPANY, PROGRAMME OR EPISODE.
LINK -> this is PANEL 15's non-inevitability argument with one instance
  substituted.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 19/22: Skill education (2023 Q2(c), 15) -- capability, not employability
"EVALUATE" -> NAME A STANDARD, APPLY IT, CONCEDE THE COUNTER-CASE, GRADE THE
  VERDICT. THE STANDARD HERE IS CAPABILITY AND AGENCY, NOT EMPLOYMENT.
        |
        v
THE POSITIVE ARGUMENT -- FOUR STEPS, STATED AT ITS BEST
  1. CAPABILITY REQUIRES EFFECTIVE MEANS, not formal opportunity alone
  2. RELEVANT SKILLS improve CONVERSION of education into WORK and AGENCY
  3. PRODUCTIVE PARTICIPATION enlarges SOCIAL CONTRIBUTION and BARGAINING POWER
  4. THEREFORE skill education CAN enhance development
        |
        v
FOUR LIMITS -- EACH ONE IS A PARAGRAPH
  1 SKILLS WITHOUT DECENT WORK -> FRUSTRATED CAPABILITY: the capacity is CREATED
    AND THEN BLOCKED. A distinctive harm, not a mere absence of benefit.
  2 NARROW EMPLOYABILITY -> displaces CRITICAL, CIVIC and HUMANISTIC education;
    equips a WORKER while DISEQUIPPING A CITIZEN
  3 UNEQUAL ACCESS -> who reaches the high-return track is itself structured by
    inherited advantage; the programme REPRODUCES the hierarchy it should relax
  4 OBSOLESCENCE -> a terminal qualification expires; the durable capability is
    the CAPACITY TO RE-LEARN
        |
        v
TWO MIRROR ERRORS
  "SKILL EDUCATION IS SUFFICIENT" ...... defeated by limit 1
  "SKILL EDUCATION IS MERELY
   INSTRUMENTAL" ....................... defeated by DIGNITY OF LABOUR --
                                         competence and self-respect are PART OF
                                         a developed life
        |
        v
THE AGENCY TEST -> DOES THE SKILL ENLARGE THE LEARNER'S **BARGAINING POWER**, OR
  ONLY HER **USEFULNESS TO AN EMPLOYER**? If only the second, the person has been
  made a more productive INSTRUMENT and not a freer AGENT -- development counted
  in the wrong informational space.
POSITION -> NEITHER SUFFICIENT NOR MERELY INSTRUMENTAL: COMBINE LIVELIHOOD,
  ADAPTABILITY, DIGNITY OF LABOUR AND CRITICAL CITIZENSHIP.
LUXURY OBJECTION -> for the workless, employability now beats citizenship later.
REPLY -> they are NOT SUBSTITUTES: a narrowly trained worker who is displaced
  loses the WHOLE of her acquired capability; general reasoning and the capacity
  to re-learn are what SURVIVE. RESIDUAL: under severe scarcity the ALLOCATION
  question is not settled by the capability framework itself.
DISCIPLINE -> NO EMPLOYMENT FIGURES, SCHEME NAMES OR OUTLAYS.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 20/22: Gandhian social development (2020 Q2(a), 20) and the Ambedkarite safeguard
"STATE AND EXAMINE" -> BOTH HALVES ARE SCORED.
DOCTRINE -> WELFARE AND MORAL ELEVATION OF ALL (sarvodaya) through SELF-RULE
  (swaraj) | NON-VIOLENCE | DECENTRALISATION | DIGNITY OF LABOUR | TRUSTEESHIP |
  RESTRAINT OF WANTS | VILLAGE-CENTRED PRODUCTION
        |
        v
THE ARGUMENT -- FIVE STEPS (an argument, not a set of ideals)
  1. UNLIMITED WANTS create DEPENDENCE, COMPETITION and VIOLENCE
  2. CENTRALISED INDUSTRIAL POWER separates PRODUCTION from HUMAN NEED and LOCAL
     CONTROL
  3. MEANS SHAPE ENDS -- violent or degrading development cannot yield a
     non-violent good society
  4. SELF-RULE requires ECONOMIC AND MORAL CAPACITIES at the LOCAL LEVEL
  5. THEREFORE development must be DECENTRALISED, NEED-ORIENTED and
     ECOLOGICALLY RESTRAINED
FOUR PRESUPPOSITIONS -> moral self-limitation is politically relevant |
  small-scale association can sustain participation | work has ethical and
  community value beyond income | change can proceed through non-violent
  conversion and constructive action
        |
        v
THREE OBJECTIONS AND REPLIES
  +---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
  | ROMANTIC VILLAGE    | villages may REPRODUCE CASTE AND PATRIARCHY.        |
  |                     | REPLY: decentralisation is defensible ONLY WITH     |
  |                     | EQUAL RIGHTS AND ANTI-DOMINATION. AMBEDKAR'S       |
  |                     | CRITICISM IS **INDISPENSABLE**, NOT OPTIONAL.      |
  +---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
  | TECHNOLOGICAL       | small-scale production cannot meet complex needs.   |
  | CONSERVATISM        | REPLY: read it as a TEST OF SCALE, CONTROL AND      |
  |                     | NEED -- not a ban on every machine.                 |
  +---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
  | TRUSTEESHIP AS      | owners retain power; obligation is voluntary.       |
  | VOLUNTARY           | REPLY: trusteeship MORALLY DELEGITIMISES ABSOLUTE   |
  | PATERNALISM         | OWNERSHIP -- but INSTITUTIONAL MECHANISMS are       |
  |                     | needed to convert obligation into ACCOUNTABILITY.   |
  +---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
TWO COMPARISONS
  GANDHI vs INDUSTRIAL MODERNISATION -> scale, productivity, mastery AGAINST
    whether scale CENTRALISES POWER, MULTIPLIES WANTS, DEGRADES LABOUR OR NATURE
    -> SYNTHESIS: adopt technology SELECTIVELY by capability, decentralised
    control, ecological cost and non-domination
  GANDHI vs AMBEDKAR ON THE VILLAGE -> SCHOOL OF SELF-RULE against
    INSTITUTIONALISED CASTE EXCLUSION -> LOCAL SELF-GOVERNMENT IS PROGRESSIVE
    ONLY WHERE CONSTITUTIONAL RIGHTS, MOBILITY AND ANTI-CASTE SAFEGUARDS
    TRANSFORM LOCAL POWER. (Bounded bridge; full caste doctrine ROUTED.)
RESTRAINT OBJECTION -> asking self-limitation of those who have little
  moralises poverty.
REPLY -> restraint targets MULTIPLIED AND MANUFACTURED WANTS, not NEED -- the
  same asymmetry as the ecological argument. RESIDUAL: the NEED/WANT BOUNDARY is
  not fixed by the doctrine, which supplies a DIRECTION and not a LINE.
VERDICT -> THE CONCEPTION SURVIVES IN A **CONDITIONED** FORM: a standing test of
  scale and control, operating inside guarantees it does not itself supply.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/22: Tribal values (2025 Q3(c), 15) -- the four tests and the classified Indian record
THE CONFLICT IS **NOT** "MODERNITY VERSUS TRADITION". IT IS FIVE QUESTIONS ABOUT
AUTHORITY AND DISTRIBUTION:
  1 WHO DEFINES BENEFIT?          4 WHOSE KNOWLEDGE COUNTS?
  2 WHO BEARS COST AND RISK?      5 ARE CONSENT AND SELF-GOVERNMENT REAL?
  3 IS LAND ONLY A COMMODITY, OR ALSO IDENTITY AND RELATION?
WHAT PROJECTS CALLED DEVELOPMENT CAN IMPOSE -> DISPLACEMENT | EXTRACTION |
  ECOLOGICAL LOSS | CULTURAL ASSIMILATION, while BENEFITS FLOW ELSEWHERE.
DESCRIPTION DISCIPLINE -> TRIBAL COMMUNITIES ARE INTERNALLY DIVERSE. AVOID
  ROMANTIC GENERALISATION. Many such conflicts DO involve community control of
  resources, ecological dependence, customary institutions, intergenerational
  continuity and collective identity.
        |
        v
THE SEVEN-STEP RECONCILIATION MODEL
  1 begin with EQUAL CITIZENSHIP and CULTURAL RECOGNITION
  2 assess CAPABILITIES GAINED **AND CAPABILITIES DESTROYED**
  3 secure INFORMED AND INCLUSIVE PARTICIPATION of the affected
  4 recognise INDIVIDUAL AND COMMUNITY RIGHTS WITHOUT FREEZING CULTURE
  5 distribute BENEFITS, BURDENS **AND DECISION-POWER** fairly
  6 preserve EXIT, DISSENT and GENDER JUSTICE within communities
  7 prefer REVERSIBLE and ECOLOGICALLY SUSTAINABLE alternatives under grave
    uncertainty
        |
        v
THE FOUR TESTS -- REQUIRED **JOINTLY**. THIS IS THE MARK-BEARING STRUCTURE.
  (i)   CAPABILITIES GAINED **AND DESTROYED** both counted, INCLUDING THE
        COMMUNITY'S OWN
  (ii)  those bearing the cost PARTICIPATE IN THE **DECISION**, not merely in
        the COMPENSATION
  (iii) IRREVERSIBLE ecological and cultural losses attract a PRECAUTIONARY
        PRESUMPTION AGAINST -- they foreclose options for PRESENT DISSENTERS and
        FUTURE GENERATIONS alike
  (iv)  the burden of ecological adjustment is NOT SHIFTED ONTO THOSE LEAST ABLE
        TO BEAR IT
  THIS CONVERTS THE SEVEN-STEP MODEL FROM DESIDERATA INTO AN ARGUED TEST.
        |
        v
INDIAN INSTITUTIONAL RECOGNITIONS -- CLASSIFY BEFORE USE
  PESA, 1996 ...................... ENACTED STATUTE extending adapted panchayat
                                    arrangements to Scheduled Areas, with a role
                                    for the GRAM SABHA
  FOREST RIGHTS ACT, 2006 ......... ENACTED RIGHTS-RECOGNITION STATUTE
                                    recognising individual AND COMMUNITY forest
                                    rights, including community rights over
                                    management and protection
  ORISSA MINING CORPORATION v.
  MINISTRY OF ENVIRONMENT &
  FOREST (2013) ................... SUPREME COURT JUDGMENT involving GRAM SABHA
                                    CONSIDERATION of community and religious
                                    claims
  ARTICLES 48A and 51A(g) ......... NON-JUSTICIABLE constitutional provisions --
                                    Directive Principle and Fundamental Duty on
                                    protection of environment, forests, wildlife
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | ENACTMENT IS NOT NOTIFICATION. NOTIFICATION IS NOT IMPLEMENTATION.    |
  | IMPLEMENTATION IS NOT CAPABILITY EXPANSION. A JUDGMENT IS NOT         |
  | LEGISLATION. A NON-JUSTICIABLE PROVISION ESTABLISHES COMMITMENT AND   |
  | NOT OUTCOME. NONE OF THEM PROVES A PHILOSOPHICAL POSITION.            |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
INTERNAL-JUSTICE TENSION -> if community authority is real, are exit and gender
  justice external impositions?
REPLY -> community rights are recognised because they protect THE CONDITIONS
  UNDER WHICH PERSONS LIVE VALUED LIVES; that justification CANNOT EXTEND to
  arrangements destroying those conditions for some members. The DIGNITY
  THRESHOLD attaches to PERSONS and is NOT THE COMMUNITY'S TO TRADE.
RESIDUAL -> deciding when an internal practice crosses the line requires an
  external judgment -- exactly the authority post-development challenges.
VERDICT -> DEVELOPMENT IS RECONCILABLE WITH TRIBAL VALUES ONLY WHEN COMMUNITIES
  ARE **AGENTS** OF DEVELOPMENT RATHER THAN OBSTACLES OR OBJECTS.


ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 22/22: Ecology, the debates, the sixteen traps, the answer spine and the PYQ ledger
ECOLOGY BLOCK A -- MORAL STANDING (three DISTINCT positions, not degrees)
  ANTHROPOCENTRIC .. human beings only; nature has INSTRUMENTAL value | WEAKNESS:
                     permits destruction where NO human interest is engaged
  BIOCENTRIC ....... all individual living beings, each with a GOOD OF ITS OWN |
                     WEAKNESS: cannot adjudicate between organisms or protect a
                     SPECIES or SYSTEM as such
  ECOCENTRIC ....... ecological WHOLES -- species, habitats, ecosystems |
                     WEAKNESS: risks SUBORDINATING INDIVIDUALS to the whole
  DISCRIMINATING QUESTION -> would it be wrong to destroy a wilderness NO HUMAN
    will ever see, use or know of? ANTHRO: no. BIO and ECO: yes, differently.
  WARNING -> DO NOT ADOPT STRONG ECOCENTRISM CASUALLY: it licenses COERCIVE
    LIMITS for systemic goods and sits badly with the capability framework.
    DEFENSIBLE POSITION: STRONG, NON-INSTRUMENTAL ANTHROPOCENTRISM SUPPLEMENTED
    BY DUTIES OF STEWARDSHIP -- stated and defended, never a "harmony" slogan.
BLOCK B -- NAESS (1973): SHALLOW fights POLLUTION and DEPLETION for the HEALTH
  AND AFFLUENCE OF THE DEVELOPED WORLD; DEEP questions the FRAMEWORK, affirms
  INTRINSIC VALUE in non-human life and RICHNESS AND DIVERSITY as values in
  themselves. THE DIFFERENCE IS **DEPTH OF QUESTIONING, NOT DEGREE OF CONCERN**.
  Objection: it can equate SUBSISTENCE with LUXURY consumption. Reply: the strong
  version distinguishes them -- which DOMESTICATES deep ecology. Say so.
BLOCK C -- PRODUCTIVISM: expanding production as the MASTER OBJECTIVE. IT APPLIES
  TO INDUSTRIAL SOCIALISM AS MUCH AS TO CAPITALISM. DEGROWTH: finite sinks and
  stocks | efficiency gains OFFSET by expanded volume | beyond a threshold output
  adds little to health, longevity, education or reported well-being IN AFFLUENT
  SOCIETIES | so PERMANENT AGGREGATE GROWTH is neither feasible nor necessary for
  them | planned reduction in THROUGHPUT around SUFFICIENCY, CARE, SHORTER
  WORKING TIME, EQUALITY | BURDEN ON HIGH-CONSUMING ECONOMIES AND CLASSES.
  ASYMMETRY -> CONTRACTION AND CONVERGENCE. NEVER PRESCRIBE DEGROWTH UNIFORMLY.
  WEAKEST LINK (concede) -> that a NON-GROWING economy can maintain employment,
  public services and social stability.
  CAPABILITY BRIDGE -> if development is CAPABILITY not OUTPUT, then beyond the
  level at which output secures capability, further growth has NO AUTOMATIC
  DEVELOPMENTAL CLAIM. Sen reaches the same conclusion by a NON-ECOLOGICAL route.
BLOCK D -- INTERGENERATIONAL JUSTICE: duties of JUSTICE, not benevolence, not to
  FORECLOSE the capacity of the unborn to meet their needs. BRUNDTLAND (1987)
  builds it into the concept -- PARAPHRASE, NEVER QUOTE. THREE PROBLEMS: the
  NON-IDENTITY PROBLEM (different policies produce different people; the person
  born into a degraded world owes her existence to the degrading policy ->
  response: IMPERSONAL principles, or ground the duty in the RESOURCE BASE);
  MOTIVATION AND RECIPROCITY (the unborn cannot vote, bargain or sanction);
  DISCOUNTING (a PURE TIME PREFERENCE is arbitrary in the way that discounting
  DISTANCE is arbitrary). SPECIFICATION -> PRESERVE OPTIONS, AVOID
  IRREVERSIBILITY. Uncertainty then argues FOR PRECAUTION, not for inaction.
BLOCK E -- ENVIRONMENTALISM OF THE POOR (GUHA and MARTINEZ-ALIER, *VARIETIES OF
  ENVIRONMENTALISM*, 1997): in the global South environmental conflict is
  typically a struggle over LIVELIHOOD -- forests, water, grazing, fisheries,
  clean air -- not a POST-MATERIAL taste for wilderness. THEREFORE ENVIRONMENT
  AND DEVELOPMENT ARE THE SAME QUESTION for such communities, and "ENVIRONMENT
  VERSUS DEVELOPMENT" IS A FRAMING AVAILABLE ONLY TO THOSE WHOSE SUBSISTENCE IS
  NOT AT STAKE. THE REAL QUESTION IS DISTRIBUTIVE: WHO GAINS, WHO BEARS THE RISK,
  WHO DECIDES, ON WHAT TERMS. Objection: it ROMANTICISES the poor. Reply: it is
  about the STRUCTURE OF CONFLICT, not ecological virtue -- and SECURE RIGHTS
  convert DEPENDENCE INTO STEWARDSHIP.
BLOCK F -- ECO-AUTHORITARIANISM: hard limits + slow, short-horizoned democracy ->
  insulate decisions. FOUR REPLIES IN ASCENDING STRENGTH: EPISTEMIC (dispersed
  local knowledge of soils, water, seasons, species) | COMPLIANCE (imposed
  measures are evaded and reversed) | RECORD (insulation removes the FEEDBACK
  that detects damage) | DISTRIBUTIVE, THE DECISIVE ONE (a burden falling on
  those least able to bear it is neither JUST nor DURABLE -> JUST TRANSITION
  conditions restructuring on PRIOR GUARANTEES of livelihood, retraining,
  compensation and participation). VERDICT: THE ECOLOGICAL CASE **STRENGTHENS**
  THE ARGUMENT FOR PARTICIPATION.
FIVE INTER-SCHOOL DEBATES
  GROWTH MODEL vs SEN ....... unit: output vs SUBSTANTIVE OPPORTUNITY | person:
    consumer/factor vs AGENT | inequality: distribute after growth vs CONVERSION
    AND FREEDOM CENTRAL | democracy: possibly instrumental vs INSTRUMENTAL AND
    CONSTITUTIVE
  SEN vs NUSSBAUM ........... evaluative space + public reasoning vs LIST +
    DIGNITY THRESHOLD
  GANDHI vs INDUSTRIAL
    MODERNISATION ........... scale/productivity/mastery vs scale as
    CENTRALISATION, MULTIPLIED WANTS, DEGRADED LABOUR OR NATURE
  GANDHI vs AMBEDKAR ........ village as SCHOOL OF SELF-RULE vs INSTITUTIONALISED
    CASTE EXCLUSION
  MODERNISATION vs
    POST-DEVELOPMENT ........ institutional transformation vs IMPOSED STANDARDS
    AND EXPERT DOMINATION -> PLURAL DEVELOPMENT UNDER UNIVERSAL CONSTRAINTS
SIX CRITICISMS AND REPLIES (with residual problems)
  capability is hard to measure -> evaluative richness beats false precision |
    residual: policy needs indicators
  universal capabilities are paternalistic -> protect opportunity, permit plural
    functionings | residual: who fixes the threshold?
  participation romanticises communities -> include dissent, rights, review |
    residual: elite capture
  sustainability blocks poor societies' growth -> affluent consumption and
    unequal burdens are the target | residual: transition costs
  Gandhianism is anti-modern -> a critique of scale, wants and domination |
    residual: complex infrastructure
  social progress is value-relative -> freedom from severe deprivation and
    humiliation have cross-cultural force | residual: reasonable disagreement
THE SIXTEEN TRAPS
  (1) growth, development, human development and progress are NOT synonyms.
  (2) never say economic development is simply "necessary and sufficient" --
      state the MATERIAL-THRESHOLD nuance. (3) Sen is NOT income plus welfare;
  capability is REAL FREEDOM and AGENCY matters. (4) capabilities are NOT
  achieved functionings. (5) a human-development composite is NOT a complete
  theory of progress. (6) technology does NOT create ethical progress. (7) skill
  education is NOT an employment statistic -- discuss capability, work quality
  and critical education. (8) do NOT romanticise tribal culture or the village.
  (9) enactment of PESA or FRA is NOT implementation or success. (10) a scheme,
  statute or judgment is NOT philosophical proof -- use it as a DATED
  INSTITUTIONAL ILLUSTRATION. (11) an indicator is NOT a theory of development --
  state its INFORMATIONAL SPACE and BLIND SPOT. (12) modernisation is NOT neutral
  convergence or an inevitable sequence -- name LINEARITY, DIFFUSION and
  EXPERT AUTHORITY. (13) not every social change is progress -- state the VALUED
  DIRECTION and permit regress, reversal and plural paths. (14) consultation is
  NOT empowerment -- empowerment requires DURABLE DECISION-POWER, RESOURCES,
  VOICE and REASONS. (15) do NOT use current social or economic figures; NO
  FIGURE IS REQUIRED. (16) do NOT import full liberalism, Marxism or Ambedkar's
  caste theory -- use the DEVELOPMENT CRITERION and ROUTE the doctrine.
THE INTEGRATED ANSWER SPINE
  1 NAME THE CONCEPTION the stem assumes (economistic | human development |
    capability)
  2 STATE THE LADDER DISTINCTION the stem turns on
  3 RUN THE STEM'S OWN LOGIC in its own vocabulary -- MODAL | CAUSAL |
    EVALUATIVE | RECONCILIATION
  4 ONE TABLE: concept grid | Sen's terms | the three orientations | the
    convergence grid
  5 ONE FULLY WORKED OBJECTION -> REPLY -> RESIDUAL PROBLEM
  6 ONE CLASSIFIED INDIAN ILLUSTRATION where the stem invites it
  7 GRADED VERDICT separating MEANS, CONVERSION CONDITIONS and FINAL VALUES
DIRECTIVE DECODER
  EXPLAIN ................. definition -> components -> distinction from the
                            neighbouring term -> example
  DISCUSS ................. conception -> rival -> objection -> reply -> verdict
  EVALUATE ................ name the standard -> apply -> concede the
                            counter-case -> verdict
  NECESSARY/SUFFICIENT? ... test necessity -> test sufficiency -> state which
                            holds and why -> verdict IN THOSE TERMS
  DO YOU AGREE THAT...? ... qualified position at the OUTSET -> reasons ->
                            counter-case -> restated position
  RECONCILE X WITH Y? ..... state what each requires -> identify the collision ->
                            specify the CONDITIONS under which both are satisfied
  DOES X LEAD TO Y? ....... mechanism -> what could break it -> verdict on the
                            conditional
PYQ LEDGER 2018-2025 -- 6 OF 112 OWNED PARTS
  2019 Q2(c) 15 -> does TECHNOLOGICAL DEVELOPMENT lead to progress in ETHICAL
    STANDARDS?
  2020 Q2(a) 20 -> STATE AND EXAMINE the GANDHIAN concept of social development
  2022 Q3(a) 20 -> does economic development ON ITS OWN lead to HUMAN
    DEVELOPMENT and SOCIAL PROGRESS?
  2023 Q2(c) 15 -> will emphasis on SKILL EDUCATION enhance development?
  2024 Q3(c) 10+5 -> is economic development NECESSARY, SUFFICIENT, BOTH OR
    NEITHER for social progress?
  2025 Q3(c) 15 -> can DEVELOPMENT be RECONCILED with TRIBAL VALUES?
  DISTRIBUTION -> 2018: 0 | 2019: 1 | 2020: 1 | 2021: 0 | 2022: 1 | 2023: 1 |
  2024: 1 | 2025: 1. NOT ONE OWNED PART ASKS FOR A DEFINITION OF DEVELOPMENT.
```
